# Sub-project 2a — Documents and Evidence Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking. In this repo, tasks run as Orca workers through the `katwork-task` skill. Each task names its **Builder** model (R20): trust-core tasks are built by Opus, the others by Sonnet. Every PR gets two independent fresh reviews, a deep reviewer (Opus) and a checklist reviewer (Sonnet, with the checklist in `CODING_STANDARDS.md`), and a verifier who tries to refute each P0 and P1 finding; the coordinator merges on the merge gate when all three ran on the exact head, nothing blocking is open and CI is green (spec §19.1). The founder approves this plan and answers its "NEEDS FOUNDER DECISION" items.

**Goal:** A person drops a folder of files (typed notes, a DOCX agenda, an XLSX budget, a PDF, later a scan) into a Space and asks a Kat for meeting minutes. The Kat reads the files, cites what it uses, and hands back a DOCX and an XLSX in which every number and date is a claim checked against its source, with a code-generated evidence report: each claim *passed*, *failed* or *unverified*, plus the missing-evidence list. The Kat then lays the minutes out in WordCraft, checks the action list in GridCraft and builds a summary deck in DeckCraft whose figures are claims too, while people watch it work in the office and one of them takes over once; all 13 Craft apps are driven the same way (R23–R25).

**Architecture:**
- SP2 is split in three (founder decision F1). **SP2a, this plan:** the document tools, the artifact store, the evidence model, the `SourceRef` and `cite` tool, the deterministic verifier, the claim judge, file deletion, KatBench, **the 13 Craft apps** (Kats drive them in the `katwork-craft` sidecar and people watch and take over, R23–R28) and Thai OCR. **SP2b** (Kat computers) and **SP2c** (memory) get their own plans; this plan leaves their seams and builds none of them.
- **The Craft apps before the demo** (R24, which moves R1's demo). The document track runs: Thai crate → artifact store → readers → spans and `cite` → writers → deterministic verifier → verification pass. Beside it, the Craft track: the forks (marks out, hardening, patent codecs) → the CI-built `katwork-craft` image → sessions behind the MCP policy proxy → the bridge and its web origin → each fork's bridge and Thai line breaking → the Kat Craft tools → `craft_save` and evidence for Craft outputs → the control lease, watching and taking over → the web panel. Then **the meeting-minutes demo** on a hand-made synthetic folder, showing them; after it, deletion, the judge, KatBench (which re-points the demo at its minutes world), the reader challenge, then OCR.
- Document tools run **in the Rust server process**, on pure-Rust libraries, with no shell and no child processes (F2): docx-rs and rust_xlsxwriter create files; KatWork's own reader on zip and quick-xml reads DOCX; umya-spreadsheet reads XLSX, but only bytes KatWork's package guard has already inflated and checked; pdf-extract, on the same single lopdf 0.46 parser KatWork pre-scans with, reads PDFs; Typst renders PDF reports. Every parse or render runs on a bounded pool of dedicated worker threads (`docs::DocPool`) with caps on decoded bytes, objects, nesting, pages, cells and time, and every reader has a fuzz target. Anything that runs a model or a native tool is **out of process**: OCR in the `katwork-ocr` container, the Craft apps in the `katwork-craft` container, which is part of the default stack and checks every XLSX output before it is saved (when it is down, saves continue and the version is marked "not checked by GridCraft", R19), the Typhoon model in a mistral.rs server.
- **Kats drive the Craft apps; people watch and take over** (R25). A Kat opens a file in an app with `craft_open`, which starts that app's MCP server in the sidecar under its own throwaway user (R22, per session) behind KatWork's **policy proxy**: every tool and command is `allow` or `forbidden` by a per-app policy that tests its own examples at start, unknown ones are forbidden, and paths stay inside the session's roots. Every step lands in a hash-chained action log, with the revision before and after and a preview, in one format for Kats and people. People watch the steps and previews live and open the same file in the app's web build, served from a separate origin and reached only through a host page that relays the KatWork bridge protocol (R26); a person who takes over holds the control lease, the Kat's run waits for them, and their save is a new version. A Craft save goes through the writers' commit path: its claims are carried by being found again in the new version, and outputs KatWork cannot read are labelled "not checked against sources". The image is built by KatWork's CI from the pinned fork commits, signed and pulled by digest (R28), and every app wraps Thai between words with KatWork's fonts (R27).
- Files live in a content-addressed blob store under one directory per workspace, with metadata in PostgreSQL. Originals are immutable; every new output version is written atomically and read back before it is recorded. An admin can delete a file, which purges its bytes and every text derived from it, through one serialized, durable path (R5).
- Evidence follows ADR-0007. Read tools mint a `source_span` for every block they extract; `cite` narrows a span to an exact quote; writer tools accept claims only as citation ids. A harness-owned verification pass runs after each run that produced an artifact: resolve each ref (a Kat's own output, hidden text, instruction-like text and deleted files are never full-quality sources), run deterministic checks (numbers and dates must match **with their subject words, periods and qualifiers**), send only disputed English claims to a judge on an admin-marked provider that can lower but never lift and whose `contradicted` alone only flags a claim (R18), append `claim_checks`, and store a code-generated `evidence_report`. A job with an artifact ends `ready`; acceptance contracts are SP3.
- File, web and OCR content is untrusted (ADR-0010). Hidden text never reaches the model, through `read_file` or a Craft app (R33; hidden as the readers detect it: text whose paint they cannot resolve counts as hidden, and ADR-0010 names what the rules cannot see), instruction-like spans cannot support a claim, and every tool call records whether its output was trusted; the verifier and the approval gate are the controls, prompt wording is a speed bump.
- Thai text lives in a new pure crate, `katwork-thai` (F5): `normalize_th()` with an offset map (never NFKC), Thai digit, number-word and scale parsing, and Buddhist-Era and fiscal-year dates, each returning value, confidence and original span.
- Evaluation is built from **gold worlds** (KatBench; K1, K2): a seeded truth layer is rendered into files by `tools/katbench-gen`, a Python tool for developers and CI only, and an independent re-read of the finished files computes every gold location; the schema, graders and statistics are Rust, in the dev-only `katbench` crate.

**Tech Stack:**
- Rust (edition 2024, workspace `rust-version` 1.97.1 unchanged): everything SP1 uses, plus `docx-rs =0.4.22` (MIT), `umya-spreadsheet =3.1.1` (MIT), `rust_xlsxwriter =0.101.0` (MIT OR Apache-2.0), `typst =0.15.1` and `typst-pdf =0.15.1` (Apache-2.0), `lopdf =0.46.0` (MIT), `pdf-extract` from a fork at a pinned revision that moves it onto lopdf 0.46 (MIT; Task 5), `zip` 9 (MIT; `default-features = false`, `features = ["deflate"]`), `quick-xml` 0.42 (MIT), `rust_decimal` 1 (MIT). The OCR container only: `oar-ocr =0.10.1` (Apache-2.0, which pins `ort =2.0.0-rc.13`, MIT OR Apache-2.0), `hayro =0.8.0` (Apache-2.0 OR MIT), `image` 0.25. Versions and licences checked on crates.io on 2026-10-24.
- Fonts: Sarabun and Noto Sans Thai static TTFs only (OFL-1.1), pinned by SHA-256 (R14), committed by Task 5, the first task that renders Thai.
- The 13 Craft apps (Apache-2.0 branch of MIT OR Apache-2.0), from the forks `Minniesse/<app>` at the commits in `docker/craft-binaries.lock`: their CLIs and web builds go into the `katwork-craft` image, which KatWork's CI builds, signs (its per-file SHA-256 list, Sigstore keyless in GitHub Actions) and publishes, and Compose pulls by digest; an override file builds the same commits on the host (R28). The sidecar's own crate adds `seccompiler` (Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause) and `sigstore` (Apache-2.0); the forks add `icu_segmenter` 2.3 (Unicode-3.0) and the shared `katwork-bridge` crate (MIT OR Apache-2.0, `Minniesse/katwork-bridge`).
- Developer and CI tools that never ship: PyThaiNLP 5.4.0 (Apache-2.0) as an offline fixture oracle; `tools/katbench-gen` (Python: Faker `th_TH` MIT, Augraphy MIT, python-docx MIT, XlsxWriter BSD-2-Clause, openpyxl MIT, pdfplumber MIT), the Typst CLI 0.15.1 and `wordcraft-cli`; cargo-fuzz on a pinned nightly. No LibreOffice anywhere (R11).
- OCR runtime: Tesseract 5 with `tha` data (Apache-2.0) and PP-OCRv5 Thai ONNX models in the `katwork-ocr` image; Typhoon OCR 1.5 (2B), opt-in, behind mistral.rs.

**Spec:** `docs/spec/katwork-design.md` (§1.2, §2 office apps, §4 Artifact, Evidence and Claim, §5, §6.5 evidence verifier, §7 proof, approvals and templates, §8 meeting minutes, §9 and §9.1, §10, §11 models and residency, §13, §14, §15, §17, §18, §19 items 2, 3 and 7). This PR amends the spec where the decisions below require it; each amendment names its decision in the PR body.

**Decisions:** ADR-0001 to ADR-0006 still apply. New: `docs/adr/0007-evidence-model-and-source-refs.md`, `docs/adr/0008-thai-ocr-engines.md`, `docs/adr/0009-kat-computer-isolation-tiers.md` (built in SP2b, recorded now), `docs/adr/0010-untrusted-content-posture.md`, `docs/adr/0011-craft-apps-in-katwork.md`. No Thai analyzer ADR: quote matching compares `match_key` strings, not search tokens, so SP2a needs no tokenizer; the analyzer is the SP2c seam.

## Founder decisions (2026-10-24)

All binding. Task and constraint lines cite them by label.

**Scope (F):**
- **F1.** SP2 splits into SP2a (this plan), SP2b (Kat computers) and SP2c (memory).
- **F2.** SP2a document tools run in-process on pure-Rust libraries (docx-rs, umya-spreadsheet, rust_xlsxwriter, Typst, lopdf), with no shell, strict per-call limits, bounded cancellation-safe workers; anything that executes code is out of process.
- **F3.** The evidence model of ADR-0007: separate claim, claim_ref and append-only claim_check records; W3C SourceRefs per file type with content hash and extractor version; Kats cite only harness-minted spans through `cite`; a verification ladder whose judge can lower or hold a claim but never lift it above unverified.
- **F4.** A `ClaimJudge` trait and a frontier judge through the model gateway, for disputed claims only; no local NLI model; a 200-claim Thai gold set (amended by R3, R4).
- **F5.** `katwork-thai`: `normalize_th()` without NFKC, with an offset map; Thai digits and number words; BE and fiscal-year dates with value, confidence and span; PyThaiNLP as an offline oracle, `_find_month` not ported. The Thai analyzer ADR only if SP2a needs search (it does not).
- **F6.** An `OcrEngine` trait, an `OcrPage` record with line boxes, a 100-page Thai bake-off, OCR last (engine order amended by R6).
- **F7.** ADR-0009 recorded now, built in SP2b.
- **F8.** Research proposals become issues only when their part starts; this plan maps each one.

**KatBench (K):**
- **K1.** The generator is a Python dev-only tool under `tools/katbench-gen/` (Faker `th_TH`, Augraphy, the OFL fonts); the schema and graders are Rust; CI pins the Python dependencies; generated fixtures are committed only if small, otherwise regenerated from a seed.
- **K2.** The SP2a Thai claim gold set is synthetic only: 100 supported claims with labels known by construction and 100 synthetic hard negatives of about 10 types, labelled `supported`, `contradicted` or `unverifiable`. The human-labelled set from real documents is deferred (Thai agency copyright; a reviewer budget).

**Review round (R):**
- **R1. Demo first** (its place amended by R24), in the order given under Architecture, with a hand-made synthetic demo folder; the Craft sidecar lands right after the demo; the demo is re-pointed at the KatBench minutes world once it exists.
- **R2. Number checks need subject words.** A number passes only if the cited quote also contains the claim's subject words (entity, metric, period), compared after `match_key` with the number masked; otherwise unverified (`subject_not_in_source`), and English claims go to the judge. Years are not quantities when they are part of a period. A claim whose text holds a quantity is number-checked whatever its kind. หมื่นล้าน and ล้านล้าน scales and XLSX trailing-comma display formats are handled.
- **R3. Judge providers.** Admins mark which providers may judge, per Space (a setting and a migration); the judge sends text only to a marked provider. Choice order: a marked provider other than the writer's; else a marked different model of the writer's provider (recorded on the check); never the writer's exact model; none marked means "needs review" (`judge_unavailable`). Thai claims are never sent to a judge. The product judge and the calibration protocol use the same vote count.
- **R4. Thai judge results are a flag, never a status change.** Thai claims get the "needs review" path, plus, from any future advisory source, an "AI found a possible mismatch" flag, with en and th copy.
- **R5. File deletion (PDPA) in SP2a.** An admin deletes a file: its blob, extracted text, span texts and citation quotes are purged; evidence rows keep ids and hashes with a `source_deleted` marker; claims citing it become unverified (`source_deleted`); ADR-0007's append-only rule allows redaction through this one audited path.
- **R6. Typhoon OCR** is served by mistral.rs if it supports the model (its loader maps the architecture; a load test confirms), else vLLM as an optional GPU profile; **PP-OCRv5 Thai is the default engine**; Typhoon is opt-in until the founder's legal read of its terms passes; it goes through the model gateway; the OCR number cap rule is an explicit, tested interface.
- **R7. Licences.** Benchmark data CC BY 4.0, code Apache-2.0.
- **R8. Snapshots.** Disk checkpoints are required (encrypted, validated); memory snapshot and fork are optional, later, microVM tier only; a Kat "tries two approaches" by restarting from a disk checkpoint. Spec §1.2's "no VM per Kat" covers code; Chromium may use a microVM when KVM exists.
- **R9. The document-tool defaults migration** (now `0015_document_tool_defaults.sql`) gives the new tools only to unedited default Kats, with a `policy_version` bump.
- **R10. Template filling and the render check move to SP3.** The render check is advisory until it passes: patched WordCraft is compared against real-Word PDFs on a Thai and English corpus; until it passes the §9.1 gate its render is a preview labelled "approximate", and acceptance uses read-back.
- **R11. No LibreOffice anywhere.** Fixtures are made with Word, Typst or WordCraft (and KatWork's own Rust writers); the generator makes PDFs with Typst and converts DOCX to PDF with `wordcraft-cli convert` built from a pinned commit; CI uses no LibreOffice image.
- **R12. Craft adoption** (the outage rule amended by R19; "built by the image itself" amended by R28; the Thai task, "SP3's first task", replaced by R27; the sidecar's scope widened by R23 and R25, and its place "right after the minutes demo" moved before the demo by R24). Craft CLIs are built from pinned commits (by the sidecar image itself, Task 15), each build's binary SHA-256 recorded, never from mutable release assets. Dev and CI: `wordcraft-cli convert` in the generator; `gridcraft-cli check --json` on every XLSX KatWork or the generator writes. Reader challengers: PdfCraft text extraction against pdf-extract, `gridcraft_xlsx::read_xlsx` (inside the package guard) against umya-spreadsheet, scored on Thai fidelity and evidence integrity; the defaults stay until a challenger wins. A new product task right after the minutes demo: the `katwork-craft` sidecar (a pinned container on an internal network with no egress, not a child process of the server), **in the product path now and in the default stack**: WordCraft previews of each DOCX output, GridCraft's check of each XLSX output before commit (originally failing closed when the sidecar is down; R19 replaces that: saves continue and the version is marked unchecked; an operator turns the sidecar off only with `KATWORK_CRAFT=off`, which logs a warning and marks versions `off`), and PdfCraft text extraction only if it wins. Thai wrapping in the Craft apps (spaces only) is SP3's first task (a WordCraft fork with VectorCraft's ICU4X dictionary segmenter, Thai tests that fail without a Thai font, an upstream PR). §9.1's bridge list is stale (SP7 seam). Licence flags go to the §9.1 licence review.
- **R13. Annotators.** The founder funds two Thai reviewers; one annotator programme covers the bake-off's real pages and the later real claim set; real pages are added when the reviewers start, synthetic only until then.
- **R14. Fonts.** Ship only Sarabun and Noto Sans Thai (OFL); TH Sarabun New/PSK waits for a rights check.
- **R15. Legal reads** (Typhoon's terms; which public Thai documents are usable) are founder actions before customer deployments, not open questions.
- **R16. Out of SP2a:** the 3D office, KatsAgentOS and other side tracks.
- **R17. The `Minniesse/pdf-extract` fork is approved.** It moves pdf-extract onto lopdf 0.46 and is created when Task 5 starts.

**Final-review round (R), 2026-10-24:**
- **R18. A judge-only `contradicted` never fails a claim**, English included. A claim that only the judge calls contradicted stays "needs review" (`unverified`, code `judge_contradicted`) with the visible "AI found a possible mismatch" flag. Only deterministic checks (number, date, cell, quote, arithmetic, amount words) can make a claim `failed`.
- **R19. When the Craft sidecar is unavailable, saves continue.** An XLSX is saved and its version marked `gridcraft_check = 'unavailable'` (a column of `artifact_versions`), shown on the Files page and in the evidence report as "not checked by GridCraft". A file GridCraft actively reports Excel would repair is still refused (`xlsx_would_be_repaired`). `KATWORK_CRAFT=off` marks versions `'off'` the same way. An outage shows the person a notice, and `/healthz` reports `"craft": "unavailable"`. Previews degrade the same way: no preview, with a label.
- **R20. Builders and reviewers** (extended the same day by the pipeline and process decisions). Claude only; Codex is no longer available. Each task carries a **Builder** line: trust-core tasks `Opus`, the others `Sonnet`. A Sonnet task names its exact files, signatures, test names with their assertions, and commands with their expected output, and leaves no choice open; it holds no trust-core code. Every PR gets two independent fresh reviews, a **deep reviewer** (Opus) and a **checklist reviewer** (Sonnet, whose list includes spec conformance, a "Spec deviations" section for plan, ADR and spec PRs, and, for plans, whether a Sonnet builder could follow each non-trust-core task with no guesses), and a **verifier** tries to refute each P0 and P1 finding before it goes back to the builder. At most three fix rounds; after the second, P3 findings become follow-up issues, while P0, P1 and security findings always block. **The founder approves each plan and answers every "NEEDS FOUNDER DECISION" item; code PRs merge on the merge gate**: the coordinator (the agent that dispatches the workers) merges a code PR only when all three ran on its exact head SHA, nothing blocking is open and CI is green, and reads the full diff of a trust-core PR first. Spec §19.1 says so (`CODING_STANDARDS.md`, the `katwork-task` and `katwork-review` skills).
- **R21. A host without gVisor keeps agentOS, with a warning** (ADR-0009). After a kill criterion fires, a host that has no gVisor (`runsc`) keeps running Kat code in agentOS until gVisor is installed, with a visible warning: to the operator at startup, and to people in every Space whose Kats run code. Code execution is not turned off.
- **R22. One shared Craft container, a throwaway user per call** (spec §9.1 "isolated per tenant"). SP2a runs one `katwork-craft` container for all workspaces. Every CLI call runs as its own throwaway user: a fresh uid and gid per call, its own temp directory with mode 0700 and no other writable path, at most 2 calls at once, and nothing kept after the call (Task 15). One container per workspace arrives with SP7; spec §9.1 says so. (Applied per MCP session from R25: Task 16.)

**Craft round (R), 2026-10-24 (R34 on 2026-10-25):**
- **R23. All 13 Craft apps are in SP2a:** WordCraft, GridCraft, PdfCraft, DeckCraft, DesignCraft, VectorCraft, FigCraft, PhotoCraft, LightCraft, CADCraft, FilmCraft, EffectCraft, SoundCraft.
- **R24. They are built before the meeting-minutes demo;** the demo moves out and shows them.
- **R25. Kats drive each app through its own MCP server or CLI inside the sandboxed `katwork-craft` sidecar;** people open the same file in the app's web build in the office, watch the Kat work live, and can take over: one active editor at a time under a control lease; while a person holds it the Kat pauses; a save makes a new version.
- **R26. The 9 apps with no host bridge** (all but GridCraft, PdfCraft, CADCraft, VectorCraft) are forked under the founder's GitHub (`Minniesse/<app>`) and get one shared KatWork bridge; upstream PRs are opened. The 4 existing bridges get a small adapter to the same protocol.
- **R27. Thai line breaking is fixed in SP2a in every app that lacks it:** ICU4X dictionary line breaking (copy VectorCraft's and PhotoCraft's approach) and KatWork's bundled Thai fonts, with Thai tests that fail without a Thai font, in the forks, with upstream PRs. This replaces SP3's "first task" (the WordCraft Thai fork) in R12 and the Seams section.
- **R28. The `katwork-craft` image is built by KatWork's CI** from the pinned fork commits and published under the founder's GitHub container registry, pulled by digest, with the build's per-binary SHA-256 list signed (Sigstore keyless in GitHub Actions) and checked at startup; `docker compose up --build` still builds locally. Amends R12's "built by the image itself".
- **R33. Hidden text never reaches the model through a Craft app either.** The founder's answer, quoted: "Read-only via read_file (Recommended)". (Applied in Tasks 16, 23, 25 and 27: when the file a Kat opens in a Craft app, or one of its inputs, holds hidden text by KatWork's own readers, the session refuses every tool and command that returns document text, and the Kat reads the file with `read_file`, which leaves hidden text out.)
- **R34. The readers' skipped parts also make a hidden-content session.** The founder's answer, quoted: "Also what readers skip (Recommended)". (Applied in Task 23: the readers' existing `EmbeddedContentIgnored` and `ActiveContentIgnored` warnings on the opened file or an input also make the session hidden-content; no reader change; PDF annotations and form values, DOCX comments, XLSX notes, PPTX speaker notes and document properties stay a named residual risk in ADR-0010.)

**Untrusted content (U), the founder-approved routing of the prompt-injection research, 2026-10-24** (ADR-0010):
- **U1.** `read_file` never returns hidden PDF or XLSX block text to the model: one line says "N hidden blocks omitted", and their spans are still minted, flagged, for evidence (Task 6).
- **U2.** "Hidden" covers text whose colour has too little contrast with what lies beneath it (white or near-white on white), text under 4 pt, and text covered by a later opaque shape or image (PDF, Task 5); white or low-contrast fonts, hidden rows, columns and sheets, and the `;;;` format (XLSX, Task 4); vanished, low-contrast and tiny runs (DOCX, Task 3).
- **U3.** `RefQuality::InstructionLike`: a span whose text matches a small English and Thai instruction lexicon, or holds Unicode tag characters or bidi controls, caps a claim citing it at `unverified` (`instruction_like_source`), and the judge never sees it. It only lowers (Tasks 6, 10).
- **U4.** `Tool::output_trust()` (`Trusted` or `Untrusted`): file, web and OCR reads are `Untrusted`; the value is recorded on `tool_calls` as a replayable taint fact, the SP3 seam for taint-aware approvals (Task 6).
- **U5.** The demo test runs a hijacked scripted Writer that cites the injected sentence and writes 9,999, in Thai and in English; the claim must be `unverified` or `failed`, never `passed` (Task 28).
- **U6.** ADR-0010, "untrusted content posture", and the KatBench-Inject trap set (carriers × language × goal) in PR CI against a compliant-victim scripted model (Task 36).

## Technical choices beyond the founder decisions

The founder decisions above do not name these choices; each line names the task that builds it. The founder approved the first eight with the plan on 2026-10-24; approving the Craft round approves the rest, from "All 13 apps are forked" on.
- **hayro** (Apache-2.0 OR MIT) rasterizes PDF pages, only inside the `katwork-ocr` container, never in the server (Task 40).
- **Tesseract with `tha` data** is the last-resort OCR engine, after PP-OCRv5, in the same container (Task 40).
- **KatWork's own DOCX reader**, on `zip` and `quick-xml`, reads DOCX; docx-rs only creates files (Task 3).
- **The qualifier rule**: a negation or comparator that a claim and its cited text do not share leaves the claim unverified (`qualifier_mismatch`); it only ever lowers a status (Tasks 10 and 11).
- **The deletion scope beyond R5**: tool-call arguments and results, their events, Kat messages that quote the file, the file's name, the quoted claim text in Kat outputs (with the list of those outputs shown to the admin for review), and the `file_in_use` refusal while a job uses the file; and one retention limit, shown in the delete dialog: while a job of the file's Space that may hold a copy of the text is still running, the deletion record keeps the removed text it must still sweep from that job (Task 30).
- **Operational limits and the watchdog exit**: the 25 MiB upload cap, the 10 GiB workspace quota, the 1 GiB free-space floor, 8 uploads at once (2 per person), the 32 KiB/s upload floor, the 4 GiB container memory limit, the document limits in `DocLimits`, the per-workspace share of the document pool, and the watchdog that exits the server with code 70 when the document pool stays wedged, so Compose restarts it (Tasks 2 and 3); the 10-minute negative cache of a read tool's `file_too_complex`, and of its `doc_timeout` only when the pool's own `call_timeout` fired, neither of them retried (Task 6); and the GridCraft check's own timeout, `KATWORK_CRAFT_CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s), with a 3 s commit reserve (Task 15). (The plan approved with these held the sandbox at 2 calls at once with a 30 s deadline, a 512 MiB `/work` and 512 processes; the Craft round changed those, and they are listed under "The sidecar's process jail" below, for its approval.)
- **cargo-fuzz on a pinned nightly** toolchain, in the CI job `fuzz-smoke` (60 s per target on PRs that touch the readers, 10 minutes nightly), never in the product build (Tasks 3, 4, 5 and 40).
- **ThaiOCRBench as an internal anchor only**: run separately, never committed or redistributed and never in the headline numbers, pending the founder's legal read (R15) (Task 43).
- **All 13 apps are forked, the four with bridges too** (R26 forks nine for the bridge): GridCraft, PdfCraft and CADCraft lack Thai line breaking (R27), and VectorCraft's marks must leave the build KatWork distributes, because its brand terms allow them only in copies of the repository and in official builds. Every app in the image comes from a `Minniesse/<app>` commit, and every gate defaults on so upstream builds are unchanged (Task 13).
- **The four existing bridges get their adapter inside the fork**, through the shared crate, so every app speaks protocol v1 and the host page needs no per-app code; their own `?host=parent` bridges stay for other hosts (Tasks 18, 20, 22).
- **"Plug-in features off" covers every app**: SoundCraft's native hosts, and the sandboxed WebAssembly plug-in commands of VectorCraft, PhotoCraft, FigCraft and EffectCraft, are compiled out of KatWork's builds, as are PdfCraft's JavaScript engine, FigCraft's sharing client and LightCraft's and EffectCraft's downloaders (Task 13).
- **The patent-codec rule as an allowlist**: KatWork's builds keep VP8, VP9, AV1, Motion JPEG, Opus, Vorbis, FLAC, ALAC, MP3, PCM and the still-image formats, and compile out H.264, HEVC (and HEIC), AAC, AC-3 and E-AC-3, ProRes, DNxHD and DNxHR, APV and MPEG-2 video (Task 14).
- **The sidecar's process jail**, with the round-4 review's fixes to R22's design: a fresh uid and gid per process from a range that does not wrap in practice, never one a process still holds; every process of that uid killed after each call, double-forked children included, before its directory goes; outputs read without following any link and only when they are regular files the program owns; inputs readable but not writable; a seccomp filter that refuses every socket, `AF_UNIX` included (abstract Unix sockets share one namespace across the container, so two sessions could meet through one, and the apps' MCP servers open none), io_uring, and all shared memory and message queues; `ipc: none`, `pids_limit` and a bounded `/work`; resource limits; `oom_score_adj` 1000; the `KILL` capability, so a deadline can end a program running as another uid; no tenant data on a command line, which every process in the container can read, so the Kat's author name reaches WordCraft over its MCP pipe; every descriptor close-on-exec and all but stdin, stdout and stderr closed before a program starts, so it inherits nothing of another session; each program in its own process session; a per-session disk cap (`KATWORK_CRAFT_SESSION_DISK_BYTES`, 256 MiB) and a per-call one (64 MiB), checked against `/work`'s size at startup; a seccomp prologue that refuses the 32-bit compat and x32 ABIs; a distroless runtime with no shell; and isolation tests that run as root inside the built image. Its limits: 2 one-shot calls at once, each with a 30 s deadline, beside the Craft sessions; a 1 GiB `/work` (`KATWORK_CRAFT_WORK_SIZE`); at most 2,048 processes in the container and 128 per program; uids drawn from 1,000,000 to 2,147,483,647, none handed out while a process still holds it (Tasks 15, 16).
- **R22 applied per MCP session, not per call**: R25's sessions keep a document open across a Kat's calls, so one throwaway uid holds a session's MCP process for the session's whole life, and every process of that uid is killed when the session closes; the one-shot previews and XLSX checks keep one uid per call (Tasks 15, 16).
- **At most 2 Craft sessions at once per install by default** (`KATWORK_CRAFT_SESSIONS`, 1–8), beside 2 one-shot checks: a further `craft_open` gets `craft_busy` until one closes; one run holds at most 2; a session closes after 600 s without a call and after 3,600 s in all (Tasks 16, 23).
- **The local image build is an override file** (this amends R28's "`docker compose up --build` still builds locally": that command now builds KatWork and pulls the Craft image by digest, and the local Craft build is `docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.craft-build.yml up --build`), `docker-compose.craft-build.yml`: a digest-pinned image cannot be a build tag, and a `build:` section in the default file would make `docker compose up --build` recompile 13 applications at every start. An unsigned image runs only with `KATWORK_CRAFT_ALLOW_LOCAL=1`, which the override sets (Task 15).
- **The startup check verifies the signed list offline**, with Sigstore's trusted root pinned in the repo and the workflow identity required; the list covers every file of the image and the check refuses one it does not name, and KatWork uses only a signed image whose list its own lock names, so neither an extra file nor an older signed image runs (Task 15).
- **The proxy's rules beyond R25's list**: requests from an app's MCP server (sampling, elicitation, roots) are refused; arguments are closed-world, so a property a rule does not name is refused, and a path-like argument the policy did not declare is refused whatever its key's case or suffix; logical `in/…` and `out/…` paths are rewritten both ways, so no model sees a session directory; calls are numbered, so a call KatWork did not number is refused, and a take-over freezes the session before it saves it (Tasks 16, 26).
- **The hidden-content session** (R33): at `craft_open`, KatWork reads the file and every input it can read for evidence (DOCX, XLSX, PDF, PPTX, text, Markdown, CSV) with its own readers, within the tool's deadline; a hidden block or hidden run in any of them, one its reader cannot read, a part the readers skip with a warning (R34), or a version a hidden-content session saved (`hidden_derived`), makes the session a hidden-content session for its whole life, its saves included, and the versions it saves carry that flag on. The proxy then refuses every rule marked `returns_text`, a field every policy rule and command rule must state (Task 16's table names them), with the tool error `craft_hidden_content`, before the app sees the call; the step is logged `refused`, and the error tells the Kat to read the file with `read_file`; the apps' error messages are withheld too, since some quote the document. Editing, saving and previews still work, but for a few editing tools whose replies echo document text (GridCraft's `set_cell`, for one; the Kat writes with `write_range`); such a session cannot save CSV, text or Markdown, its `craft_save` receipt gives uncited figures by location only, and GridCraft's grid facts give it a cell count and no cell text, since the session's own edits can turn hidden text visible. In a normal session, GridCraft's grid facts come from KatWork's XLSX reader over a snapshot of the session's workbook, visible cells only (Tasks 16, 23, 25).
- **`kwbundle`**, a stored zip with a manifest, is the document of LightCraft, FilmCraft and EffectCraft, whose projects are several files (Task 16).
- **One Craft web origin for every app in SP2a** (per-session origins stay SP7's), on a different host and a different site from KatWork's (a sibling subdomain is refused unless the operator sets `KATWORK_CRAFT_WEB_SAME_SITE=1`), checked at startup: `http://127.0.0.1:3002` beside the local `http://localhost:3000`, and with HTTPS a host name under another registrable domain; **without one, people watch through previews and the action log but cannot open the app**; its server refuses service-worker scripts and its host page unregisters any service worker before it frames an app, and what one origin still shares between openings (storage, `BroadcastChannel`, WebRTC) is a named residual risk until SP7's per-session origins (Task 17).
- **Protocol v1's details**: `kw:*` message names, a fresh token per opening in the URL fragment, a frame with no downloads, pop-ups, dialogs or forms, and the threat model that the origin, not the token, protects KatWork (Task 17).
- **Printing only through the Files page**: the app frame allows no downloads, pop-ups, dialogs or forms, so a person prints a saved version from its Files page download, never from inside an app (Task 17).
- **The Craft tools' shapes beyond R25's list**: `craft_open` also takes `inputs`, a `template` and a page of the tool list; `craft_call` is `WorkspaceRead`, because its effect lives only in the session; `craft_preview` gives the Kat text facts and the image only to the people watching, because SP1's tool results are text; the action log stores ids, hashes and code-built summaries, never document text or file names (Task 23).
- **Which default Kats get which apps** (R9's rule, a second migration after 0015; each grant is a `craft.<app>` entry of the Kat's tool list): Researcher PdfCraft, FilmCraft and SoundCraft; Writer WordCraft, PdfCraft, DeckCraft, DesignCraft, FigCraft, VectorCraft, PhotoCraft, LightCraft and EffectCraft; Analyst GridCraft, DeckCraft, VectorCraft and CADCraft; Scheduler GridCraft; Reviewer WordCraft, GridCraft, PdfCraft and DeckCraft; Lead none; and the default Writer's instructions gain one sentence on working in a Craft app. The document-tool defaults migration moves, unchanged, from the demo task to Task 23 (Task 23).
- **The Craft media types and their sniff rules**; Craft documents are opaque to KatWork, parsed only by their app in the sidecar (Task 23).
- **Claim relocation**: a claim is found again by its marker, else by its exact text found once, else it is lost; a person adds no claims; a Kat's save may version an output of another job of the Space only through the lease (Task 25).
- **A person's save that cannot version the file makes one new input**: saving an input, or a file not in the app's document format (a CSV in GridCraft, Markdown in WordCraft), creates `<stem> (edited).<document ext>`, so the original stays as it was; the lease and the panel move to it, and the person's later saves version it (Tasks 25, 26).
- **Craft outputs KatWork cannot read are labelled "not checked against sources"**: images, vector art, CAD drawings, video, audio and the apps' own formats are saved as versions with no evidence check, and a save in those formats takes no claims (Task 25).
- **The pause is SP1's `needs_input`**: a take-over stops the Kat's run at its next tool boundary as `needs_input` (`human_has_control`), and the hand-back posts a message **as the person** who handed back, with the hand-off note, which starts the follow-up job through SP1's path; SP3's resume will make it the same job (Task 26).
- **A take-over first saves the Kat's unsaved work** as a Kat version, so the person edits what they watched; it skips that save, with a notice, when the session's log is not complete or the file is an input or not in the app's document format (Task 26).
- **Lease timers**: a person's lease ends after 600 s without activity and after 3,600 s in all, and only an edit or "Keep editing" counts as activity; an expiry posts a notice, never a message on the person's behalf (Tasks 26, 27).
- **The app shows saved versions; the step feed and previews show the live revision**: "Open in app" loads the latest saved version read-only and reloads on each save, so a person watches the Kat's unsaved work through the feed and its previews, not in the app (Tasks 26, 27).
- **Watching and taking over are open to members by default**: any member may watch, and any member may take over unless a Space admin limits taking over to admins (Tasks 26, 27).
- **A person can open any file in its Craft app, not only one a Kat is working on**: "Open in app" on the Files page shows a version read only, and "Edit in app" takes the lease of a file nobody holds, with the same saving and handing back as a take-over (Tasks 26, 27, 29).
- **Deletion and the Craft apps**: deleting a file also purges its Craft step previews, and is refused (`file_in_use`) while a Kat's Craft session or a person holds the file (Task 30).
- **Previews per app**: a page image for most, the visible cells for GridCraft as KatWork's XLSX reader sees them (it renders no image headlessly), a session summary for SoundCraft; a session keeps its last 50 step previews, which do not count toward the storage quota (Tasks 16, 23).
- **Thai beyond line breaking where an app needs it**: CADCraft shapes Thai runs with HarfRust, LightCraft shapes and wraps its rendered text, PhotoCraft turns its dictionary breaking on in the web build, and SoundCraft keeps Thai marks in file names (Tasks 20–22).
- **One Thai gate fixture for every fork**, two paragraphs on which ICU4X's dictionary and PyThaiNLP agree (Task 18).
- **The demo's Craft part is a second job**, within SP1's 24-step limit (Task 28).

## Global Constraints

Every Global Constraint of the SP1 plan (`docs/plans/2026-10-22-sp1-core-runtime.md`) still applies; `CODING_STANDARDS.md` makes them review rules. The ones SP2a work meets most, restated, then the new ones:

- **Licence.** Apache-2.0 core, no GPL, AGPL or LGPL code in anything that ships. `cargo deny check` runs in CI with `deny.toml`'s allowlist (`Apache-2.0`, `BSD-3-Clause`, `CDLA-Permissive-2.0`, `ISC`, `MIT`, `Unicode-3.0`, `Zlib`).
  - Task 32 adds exactly what Typst's tree needs: `BSD-2-Clause` to the allowlist (`arrayref`, `kamadak-exif`, `mutate_once`), and exceptions for `roman-numerals-rs` (`0BSD`) and `ar_archive_writer` (`Apache-2.0 WITH LLVM-exception`).
  - KatWork's own `zip` dependency turns default features off, because its defaults pull `libbz2-rs-sys` (`bzip2-1.0.6`) and `ppmd-rust` (`CC0-1.0 OR MIT-0`). docx-rs and umya-spreadsheet depend on `zip` 8.6 with `deflate` only, so the lock file holds zip 8 and zip 9; `deny.toml` has no multiple-versions ban and none is added.
  - Task 5 adds a `[sources]` section allowing exactly the pdf-extract fork's git URL.
  - Task 15's `katwork-craft` crate adds `seccompiler` and `sigstore` (`default-features = false`, with only what offline bundle verification needs); if either tree needs a licence outside the allowlist, the task stops and asks rather than adding an exception. The 13 Craft apps are separate builds from their forks, outside KatWork's Cargo workspace; their licences are recorded per app in ADR-0011 and `NOTICE` (MIT OR Apache-2.0 code; MPL-2.0 crates used unmodified: symphonia and CADCraft's DWG crate).
- **The harness never runs model output through a shell; checks run as argv arrays; tool ids are matched against a registry** (spec §5.1, §13). SP2a adds no shell anywhere and **no child process of the server**. Child processes run only inside their own containers: Tesseract in `katwork-ocr` (Task 40), the Craft CLIs and MCP servers in `katwork-craft` (Tasks 15 and 16), each as an argv array with a cleared environment and an allowlist, each MCP server behind the policy proxy. Developer and CI tools (the generator, the Typst CLI, the Craft CLIs in CI, cargo-fuzz) are outside the product path.
- **Document tools are in-process, bounded and cancellation-safe** (F2).
  - Every parse or render runs through `docs::DocPool::run` on one of `KATWORK_DOC_WORKERS` (default 2) dedicated threads with a 64 MiB stack, never on an async task or an unbounded `spawn_blocking`.
  - A timed-out or cancelled call returns at once. Its thread keeps working only until the job's next deadline check: every guard walk loop and capped decoder reads the job's deadline from a thread-local and stops with `Timeout`. The pool never runs more jobs than threads.
  - **No workspace holds the whole pool.** A workspace runs at most `max(1, workers − 1)` pool jobs at once; its further jobs queue while another workspace's job can still start. A read tool whose result is `doc_timeout` or `file_too_complex` is not retried by the loop. `file_too_complex` is cached for 10 minutes per workspace, SHA-256 and extractor, and so is a `doc_timeout` for which the pool's own `call_timeout` fired (counted from when a worker picked the job up), so a rerun cannot loop on one hostile file; a `doc_timeout` caused by the caller's own deadline, while the job was queued or running, is never cached (Tasks 3 and 6).
  - Parser work is bounded by the reader, not only the input: every decompression goes through a capped decoder, every object or element graph walk has a visited set and a depth cap, and every per-document count (entries, objects, operators, cells, blocks) has a cap checked before the library builds its model.
  - Memory has two backstops: the caps below, and a container memory limit on the `katwork` service (`KATWORK_MEMORY_LIMIT`, default `4g`, Task 3).
  - **A wedged pool restarts the process.** When every pool thread has run one job for more than three call timeouts, `/healthz` answers 503 `doc_pool_wedged`. Compose does not restart a container that is only unhealthy ([Docker restart policies](https://docs.docker.com/engine/containers/start-containers-automatically/)), so a watchdog thread (Task 3) exits the process with code 70 once the wedge has lasted one more call timeout, and `restart: unless-stopped` starts it again. The task leases the process held expire and SP1's reclaim hands their tasks on; open transactions roll back when their connections close.

  | `DocLimits` field | Default |
  |---|---|
  | `max_input_bytes` | 26,214,400 (25 MiB, the upload cap) |
  | `max_unzipped_bytes` (sum over a package) | 209,715,200 (200 MiB) |
  | `max_entry_ratio` (uncompressed ÷ compressed, entries above 1 MiB uncompressed) | 100 |
  | `max_zip_entries` | 2,000 |
  | `max_xml_depth` | 256 |
  | `max_cells` (XLSX, non-empty) | 250,000 (Task 4 measures umya's peak memory at this cap) |
  | `max_shared_strings` | 1,000,000 |
  | `max_pages` (PDF) | 500 |
  | `max_stream_bytes` (one decoded PDF stream: content, Form, font, CMap, ICC profile or any other) | 16,777,216 (16 MiB) |
  | `max_decoded_bytes` (one budget per PDF: load-time object-stream reservations plus every stream the pre-scan decodes) | 268,435,456 (256 MiB) |
  | `max_objstm_bytes` (one decoded object stream at load time; each reserves this much of `max_decoded_bytes`) | 2,097,152 (2 MiB) |
  | `max_object_streams` (per PDF) | 64 |
  | `max_pdf_objects` (counted during load, object streams included) | 500,000 |
  | `max_xobject_depth` / `max_page_tree_depth` | 8 / 32 |
  | `max_content_ops` (per PDF) | 2,000,000 |
  | `max_blocks` (per extraction) | 50,000 |
  | `max_slides` (PPTX, Task 24) | 2,000 |
  | `max_image_pixels` | 40,000,000 |
  | `max_output_bytes` (rendered file) | 52,428,800 (50 MiB) |
  | `max_output_pages` (rendered PDF) | 200 |
  | `min_visible_pt` (text below this effective size is hidden; PDF, DOCX, XLSX) | 4.0 |
  | `min_contrast` (text whose WCAG 2 contrast ratio against what lies beneath it is below this is hidden) | 1.2 |
  | `min_text_alpha` (PDF text whose painting alpha, `ca` for fill or `CA` for stroke, is below this is hidden) | 0.1 |
  | `call_timeout` (a pool job's cap, counted from when a worker picks the job up; a tool call also stops at its own deadline, whichever is first) | 30 s |
  | `wedge_exit_after` (how long `wedged()` must stay true before the watchdog exits the process) | equal to `call_timeout` |
  | `cache_bytes` (`read_file`'s extraction cache, keyed by workspace, SHA-256 and extractor) | 67,108,864 (64 MiB) |

- **Library roles** (F2, R10, R12). In SP2a no library edits a file a person supplied. docx-rs creates DOCX, rust_xlsxwriter creates XLSX, Typst creates PDF reports. DOCX is read by KatWork's own reader on zip and quick-xml, XLSX by umya-spreadsheet behind the package guard, PDF by pdf-extract on the single lopdf 0.46 parser. docx-rs and umya-spreadsheet rebuild a file from their own model and drop what they do not model ([docx-rs #597](https://github.com/bokuweb/docx-rs/issues/597), [umya-spreadsheet #364](https://github.com/MathNya/umya-spreadsheet/issues/364)); filling a customer's template, editing workbooks and lopdf's constrained PDF edits move to SP3, with the template work. pdf-extract and umya-spreadsheet stay the defaults until a Craft challenger wins (Task 39).
- **New XLSX files carry no formulas.** rust_xlsxwriter stores 0 as every formula's result unless a result is set ([docs](https://rustxlsxwriter.github.io/formulas/results.html)); SP2a writes values only, and a cell string starting with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` is written as a string. Every XLSX output is checked by GridCraft in the `katwork-craft` sidecar before it is committed (Task 15). A file GridCraft reports Excel would repair is refused (`xlsx_would_be_repaired`). When the sidecar cannot answer, the save continues and the version's `gridcraft_check` is `unavailable` (R19); with `KATWORK_CRAFT=off` it is `off`. Either way the Files page and the evidence report say "not checked by GridCraft". The sidecar is in the default `docker-compose.yml`; `KATWORK_CRAFT` accepts only `on` (default) and `off`.
- **Thai text.** `katwork_thai::normalize_th` is the only normalization for matching; plain NFKC is never applied, because it splits SARA AM (U+0E33) into U+0E4D U+0E32. Every offset KatWork stores counts Unicode code points in a stated coordinate space (ADR-0007) and never splits a grapheme cluster; byte offsets exist only inside a function.
- **Evidence rules** (ADR-0007, F3, R2–R5).
  - Kats never supply a locator: claims carry citation ids only, and citation ids come only from `cite` over harness-minted spans.
  - A Kat's own output, hidden text, instruction-like text, a suspect text layer, low-confidence OCR and a deleted file are never full-quality sources: a claim citing one is at most unverified.
  - A number or date passes only with its subject words in the cited quote and its period equal in meaning (BE and CE writings of a year are one year; a fiscal year is not a calendar year); a quantity is number-checked whatever the claim's kind. A negation or comparator (ไม่, ไม่เกิน, ไม่ต่ำกว่า, อย่างน้อย, ประมาณ, เกือบ, not, no more than, at least, about, approximately, under, over, up to, and the rest of Task 10's list) that the claim and its source do not share leaves the claim unverified (`qualifier_mismatch`).
  - The evidence report is generated by code. `claim_checks` and `evidence_reports` are append-only, enforced by a trigger, except the audited redaction of a deleted file.
  - A judge can lower or hold a claim, never lift it, and its `contradicted` alone only flags a claim (R18); it runs only on admin-marked providers, never on the writer's exact model, never on Thai claims, and never on a claim citing a span that is not full quality.
- **Untrusted content** (ADR-0010, U1–U6). File, web and OCR content is data, never instructions, and the harness assumes the model can be hijacked by it: hidden text, as the readers detect it, never reaches the model (ADR-0010 names what they cannot see), through `read_file` or through a Craft app (R33: a session on a file that holds hidden text, or parts the readers skip with a warning, R34, or that a hidden-content session saved, refuses every tool and command that returns document text); instruction-like spans cannot support a claim; each tool declares `output_trust()` and the loop records it on `tool_calls`. No guarantee rests on a prompt.
- **Tool effects.** `SideEffect::changes_state()` is false only for `Read` and `WorkspaceRead` (Task 6). `list_files`, `read_file`, `cite`, `check_artifact`, `craft_open`, `craft_call`, `craft_preview` and `craft_close` are `WorkspaceRead` (a Craft call changes only its session's in-memory document, which closes with the run); `write_docx`, `write_xlsx`, `write_pdf_report` and `craft_save` are `Draft`. A `Draft` tool writes its receipt, `artifact_versions.idempotency_key = ctx.idempotency_key` under a unique constraint, in the same transaction as its effect, and returns the stored result when the key comes again (SP1 Task 8's rule for non-Read tools).
- **Tool results are structured.** Read and check tools return JSON lines, one JSON object per block or finding, so document text can never start a line the Kat would read as harness output. `read_file`'s first line says the content is file content, and so do `craft_open`'s and `craft_call`'s for an app's replies (Task 23); `list_files`, `cite` and `check_artifact` carry file text (names, quotes, values) only inside named JSON string fields. The loop cuts every result to 8,000 Unicode scalars; `read_file`, `list_files` and `craft_open` page at 7,000 and say where the next page starts, and `craft_call` cuts an app's reply under 7,000.

- **Storage.** The workspace quota and the 1 GiB free-space floor (Task 2's `check_space`) apply to uploads and to a person's Craft saves, which stream into staging under the upload limiter (Task 26); a Kat's writes, `craft_save` included, are bounded by their output caps (50 MiB) and the quota does not refuse them, as for Task 9's writers. Derived files are bounded apart and not counted in the quota: Task 15's preview cache, and a Craft session's last 50 step previews, which are not written below the free-space floor (Task 23); all are removed with their file (Task 30).
- **No NUL in Postgres text.** Extracted text, quotes and claim text have every U+0000 replaced with U+FFFD before they are stored (SP1 rule).
- **Tenant isolation.** Every new tenant-owned table has `UNIQUE (id, workspace_id)` and references its parents with composite foreign keys `(parent_id, workspace_id)`. Blobs live under one directory per workspace, so content addressing never crosses tenants. A request or tool asking for another workspace's file, span, citation, report, Craft session, step or lease gets `not_found`, and a tool asking for a file of another Space of the same workspace gets the same; a Craft tool naming a session of another run, Space or workspace gets `craft_session_unknown`, exactly as for a session that does not exist (Task 23).
- **Roles before bodies.** File routes and the Craft watch routes need workspace membership; deletion, judge settings and Craft settings need an admin; taking over a file needs the Space's `craft_takeover_role` (Task 26); each checked by the `Tenant` extractor before the body is read. Axum captures are named `{workspace_id}`, `{space_id}`, `{artifact_id}`, `{version}`, `{session_id}` and `{seq}`.
- **Request-path transactions and writes go through `db::guarded`.** Acquire the guard before `BEGIN` or any statement and keep it until the operation completes; set `db::set_lock_timeout(conn, config.lock_timeout)` before row locks. `clippy.toml` bans `sqlx::Pool::{begin, acquire, begin_with, try_begin, try_acquire, try_begin_with}` and `sqlx::Acquire::{begin, acquire}`; an exception needs `#[allow(clippy::disallowed_methods, reason = "…")]` and a reviewer's agreement, and a worker exception must drain SQL on cancellation.
- **Cancellation (#48) applies to every new request path.**
  - A database future is never dropped mid-flight: the upload, list, download, preview, evidence, deletion, judge-settings and Craft routes, every new tool, the verification pass, the judge's model-call writes, the OCR and Craft clients' writes run their SQL inside `db::guarded` (or, for the judge, the worker's reviewed drain exception that `ledger::record_model_call` already relies on).
  - Blob, document, OCR, Craft and model I/O stays outside the guarded scope, except a blob commit's rename, a blob removal's unlink and a preview cache write, which run under their locks (Lock order).
  - Every new database entry path has a named cancellation test that drops the future **during a statement**, using Task 2's helper `cancel_sql::drop_during_statement` (the server as the barrier: a held `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` table lock, a `pg_locks` poll for this operation's waiting backend, then a `pg_stat_activity` check that the backend ended idle), then calls `assert_pool_clean` and an independent committed-write probe.
- **Lock order** (SP1, extended). Every transaction that waits for row locks takes them in this order: workspace row → **Space row** (new; only the judge-settings and craft-settings routes lock it, after the workspace row `FOR SHARE`, `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`; the judge reads it without a lock, Task 31) → job row → task row, then its `task_leases`, `runs` and `tool_calls` rows → thread row → Kat row, then its `kat_spaces` rows → **artifact rows** (new, ascending id) → **Craft lease rows** (new, by artifact id; Tasks 23, 25, 26) → **Craft session rows** (new, ascending id; their action-log appends lock them, Task 23) → **evidence and event rows** (new; only the deletion path locks them, in ascending primary key within each table: `id`, or `(thread_id, seq)` for `events`). A take-over, hand-back or expiry locks only the workspace row (`FOR SHARE`), the file's artifact row (`FOR SHARE`, the artifact lock contract), its lease row and its sessions' rows, so it never waits on a job, task or thread lock; so does a take-over's checkpoint (`CraftAuthor::Checkpoint`, Task 25), which adds only the artifact and claim rows of a commit. What they post afterwards runs in its own transactions, after their commit: the hand-back's message through `pipeline::submit` (which takes the thread lock as any message does) and the expiry's and checkpoint's System notices. Every path that closes or kills a Craft session locks its lease row before its session row (Task 23). Within a class, rows lock in ascending id. `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` when the transaction updates the row, `FOR SHARE` when it only reads it; never `FOR UPDATE`, never an upgrade from `FOR SHARE`, no row deletes.
  - The evidence tables (`source_spans`, `citations`, `claims`, `claim_refs`, `claim_checks`, `evidence_reports`, `ocr_pages`) are insert-only, except the deletion path's redaction.
  - **The artifact lock contract** (R5, Task 30). Every transaction that mints spans from a file, cites a span, records claims citing a file, or stores a verification pass's report and checks first takes that source artifact's row `FOR SHARE` and checks `deleted_at`: `read_file` and `cite` refuse a deleted file (`not_found`, `span_unknown`), writers refuse it (`citation_unknown`), and the verification pass treats its refs as `Deleted`, with blank text, and stores `[deleted]` as the text of every claim citing it and `{"redacted": true}` as those claims' check details, before it inserts anything; the pass also takes the row of the artifact it verifies and stores nothing when that one is deleted (Task 12). A writer versioning an output takes that output's row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` and refuses a deleted one (`not_found`). Deletion takes the artifact row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, right after the workspace row and before its in-use check, so each of those either commits before the deletion (and is then redacted by it) or sees the file deleted.
  - A `Draft` tool's transaction is `guards::authorize_write` (workspace, task and Kat share locks) → the receipt check (a plain read: a replay returns the stored receipt, even when a cited source was deleted since) → `store::lock_artifacts_on` (Task 2), which locks the output's row and every cited source's row **in one ascending pass**, the output `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` and the sources `FOR SHARE`, and an id that is both only once, `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` (a Kat that cites its own earlier version never upgrades a lock) → `commit_blob_on` → inserts. It never takes a job, run, tool-call or thread lock, so the SP1 Task 8 lock-order requirement holds: evidence events are appended by `job::conclude`, which takes the thread lock in its own transaction.
  - **The deletion path's redaction updates** come after the artifact row, and touch only rows of `tool_calls`, `events` and `messages` (and the evidence rows) of **finished** jobs. No other transaction locks a finished job's rows, so this exception to the order cannot deadlock. Rows of the jobs of the file's Space that were still unfinished when the deletion committed (none of them references the file, by the in-use check under the artifact lock, but a `list_files` result may name it) are swept by the deletion's `finish` once each of those jobs has finished, under the same rule; when none is left, every job of that Space that finished after the commit is swept too, and the jobs of that Space that started after the commit and are still unfinished then are waited for and swept the same way, in further rounds until a round finds none (Task 30).
  - **Blob commit and blob removal** run inside a guarded transaction that holds `pg_advisory_xact_lock(blob_lock_key(workspace_id, sha256))` (Task 2), so a removal (Task 30) and an upload or write of the same bytes never interleave. The rename, the unlink and Task 15's preview cache write (under the artifact row's `FOR SHARE`) are the only file I/O allowed inside a transaction; staging (writing and fsyncing the temp file) stays outside.
- **Migrations** are append-only; the next free number is 0011 (`0010_runs_job_id.sql` is the last on main). SP2a adds, in merge order: `0011_artifacts.sql` (Task 2), `0012_source_spans.sql` (Task 6), `0013_citations_claims.sql` (Task 7), `0014_evidence_reports.sql` (Task 12), `0015_document_tool_defaults.sql` (Task 23, moved from the demo task unchanged, R9), `0016_craft_sessions.sql` (Task 23), `0017_craft_evidence.sql` (Task 25), `0018_craft_takeover.sql` (Task 26), `0019_file_deletion.sql` (Task 30), `0020_judge_providers.sql` (Task 31), `0021_ocr_pages.sql` (Task 41). No other task adds one. If two tasks merge out of this order, the second renumbers before it merges.
- **English and Thai together.** Every user-facing string comes from `web/src/i18n/{en,th}.json`; API errors are JSON `{"code": "<stable_id>"}`; every evidence detail code has copy in both files (Task 29's test). User-facing copy calls a passed check "checked against the source", never "verified true", and evidence views carry the trust line "AI ร่างและตรวจสอบเบื้องต้น — คุณตรวจทานและอนุมัติ" (spec §3).
- **New error codes**: `file_name_invalid` (400), `file_type_unsupported` (415), `file_encoding_unsupported` (415), `too_many_uploads` (429), `upload_too_slow` (408), `storage_full` and `storage_quota_exceeded` (507), `blob_corrupt` and `span_mismatch` (500), `artifact_immutable` (409), `file_in_use` (409), `judge_provider_invalid` (400), `preview_unavailable` (503), `octet_stream_required` (415, an upload whose body is not `application/octet-stream`), `doc_pool_wedged` (503, `/healthz` only), `craft_lease_held`, `craft_lease_lost`, `craft_version_conflict` and `craft_hidden_content` (409), `craft_web_unavailable` (503); `body_too_large` (413) is reused. Every one of them except `doc_pool_wedged`, which only `/healthz` returns, has en and th copy: Task 29's table, `reason.preview_unavailable` (Task 15), the `reason.craft_*` lines (Task 26), `reason.file_in_use` (Task 30) and `reason.judge_provider_invalid` (Task 31). New tool error texts (sent to the model, never shown as UI copy): `file_too_large`, `file_too_complex`, `file_malformed`, `file_encrypted`, `doc_timeout`, `span_unknown`, `quote_not_in_span`, `quote_ambiguous`, `citation_unknown`, `artifact_not_writable`, `read_back_failed`, `xlsx_would_be_repaired`, `ocr_unavailable`, `text_too_long`, `file_not_readable`, `human_has_control`, the Craft session codes of Task 23 (`craft_unavailable`, `craft_busy`, `craft_app_not_granted`, `craft_app_unavailable`, `craft_format_unsupported`, `craft_file_busy`, `craft_session_limit`, `craft_session_unknown`, `craft_session_closed`, `craft_tool_forbidden`, `craft_command_forbidden`, `craft_path_forbidden`, `craft_argument_forbidden`, `craft_hidden_content`, `craft_timeout`, `craft_app_failed`) and Task 25's (`craft_claim_not_found`, `craft_claims_unsupported_format`, `craft_output_too_large`, `craft_output_invalid`); Task 27 gives each one copy for the watch feed. New System notice codes, each also a `ReasonCode` whose copy is `reason.<code>`: `review_before_rerun` (Task 8), `craft_unavailable` (Task 15), `human_has_control`, `craft_lease_expired` and `craft_checkpoint_skipped` (Task 26, which also gives `NoticeMeta` typed `params` for the person, file, app and version they name).
- **Secrets.** New settings come from the environment, and `Config`'s `Debug` redacts `KATWORK_OCR_VLM_API_KEY`. The OCR and Craft containers get an env allowlist, never the server's environment.
- **Data residency** (spec §11, §13). The judge sends text only to providers an admin marked for the Space, which must also be allowed by the Space's residency. The Typhoon model is a model-gateway entry with its declared residency (ADR-0005's rule: declared, never inferred from a URL).
- **KatBench** (K1, R7, R11). Like Task 1's PyThaiNLP fixture script, `tools/katbench-gen` is an exception to "all KatWork code is Rust": a Python tool for developers and CI only, never in the product image or a Rust crate, installed in its own container from a hash-pinned lock (`pip install --require-hashes`). The schema, graders and statistics are Rust, in the dev-only `katbench` crate (`publish = false`; never a normal (non-dev) dependency of `katwork` or `katwork-server`, whose test suites use it as a dev-dependency (Tasks 35, 36 and 38), and the product image builds `-p katwork-server` only). Gold comes from a world: facts first, files second, every gold location computed by re-reading the finished files. Small worlds are committed; bigger sets are regenerated from their committed seed. SP2a gold sets are synthetic only. The generator makes PDFs with the Typst CLI and converts DOCX to PDF with `wordcraft-cli convert`.
- **Fictional data** (K1; applies to the demo folder too). 13-digit identifiers fail the Thai ID checksum on purpose, phone numbers start with `000`, email addresses use `example.com`, surnames are composed from syllables and checked against Faker's `th_TH` surname list (a small pool of real surnames), organization names come from a fictional list with no real agency, university or hospital, and every generated file carries a canary string.
- **Publishing benchmark data** (R7). Code under the repo's Apache-2.0 licence; worlds, gold and synthetic fixtures under CC BY 4.0 (`bench/katbench/DATA-LICENSE.md`). Nothing derived from a real third-party document enters the repo: real OCR pages and their corrected text stay in the operator's private bench directory, listed by URL, licence and SHA-256.
- **Craft binaries** (R12, R28). The 13 apps' CLIs and web builds are built from source, from the forks `Minniesse/<app>` at the commits in `docker/craft-binaries.lock` (with the `katwork-bridge` commit), by KatWork's CI, which signs the image's per-file SHA-256 list (Sigstore keyless) and publishes the image; Compose pulls it by digest (`docker/craft-image.lock`), and the sidecar re-hashes every file of the image, refuses one the list does not name and verifies the signature at startup, and KatWork uses only the image its own lock names (Task 15). They are never downloaded from release assets (which are mutable), never `craft-launcher`, never auto-updated, and an image is never built from a fork whose Thai gate test fails. A local build (`docker-compose.craft-build.yml`) is unsigned and runs only with `KATWORK_CRAFT_ALLOW_LOCAL=1`. Every app in the image, its CLI and its web build alike, is built with its KatWork features (the lock's `features` and `web_features`, Tasks 13, 14 and 17): no plug-in host, script engine, network client, model download, collaboration client or patent-pool codec.
- **Craft apps** (R23–R27). A Kat reaches a Craft app only through the Craft tools (Tasks 23 and 25), and an app's MCP server only through the policy proxy (Task 16): one process per session, under its own uid, launched from its policy file with its automation roots where it has them and never with `--connect`, `--bridge`, `--control`, `--attach`, `--launch`, `--lazy` or `--gpu`; VectorCraft always with `--headless`; no tenant data (the Kat's author name included) on its command line. Every tool and command is `allow` or `forbidden` in a policy whose examples are tested at startup; unknown ones are forbidden; paths stay inside the session's `in/` and `out/`. A session on a file or input that holds hidden text, that KatWork's readers cannot read among the formats they read, that holds parts they skip with a warning (R34), or that a hidden-content session saved, refuses every rule its policy marks `returns_text` (R33, Tasks 16, 23 and 25). Every step is in the hash-chained action log, with no unlogged mode and no document text. Craft results are untrusted content. One editor at a time: the control lease (Tasks 23 and 26) decides who may change a file, writers included (a writer refuses a file a Craft session or a person holds), and every save is a new version through the commit path (Task 25). The web builds run only on the separate Craft web origin, reached only through the host page's relay (Task 17).
- **Craft fork changes** (R26, R27; Tasks 18–22). In each fork, one PR per app, with its upstream PR:
  - **KatWork mode** (`Bridge::from_location(wake)` is `Some`, from `?katwork=1`): the app starts with no document; keeps nothing in browser storage (each task's table names every storage layer the web build has and how KatWork mode turns it off: no `localStorage`, IndexedDB, OPFS, Cache Storage, service worker, Web Lock or crash-recovery snapshot); does not install its file pickers, drop targets, downloads, print or page API (the table names each); its own host bridge, where it has one, stays off.
  - **`impl BridgeApp`** in the web crate's `src/katwork.rs`: `load` opens the bytes as the app's document through the code its file picker uses; `save` returns the document in its document format (Task 16's recipes) through the code its Save uses, without a download; `dirty` comes from the document's unsaved flag and `revision` counts the history pushes and undo and redo steps the table names (never a counter that moves on a selection or a redraw); `set_read_only(true)` sets a read-only flag that **the input gate** checks at every place the table names (the engine's command entry and the paths that change the document outside it), so every editing input and command is refused, and shows `katwork_bridge::copy::read_only` (plus the app's own viewer mode where the table names one); `lease_changed` shows a one-line banner at the place the table names, `copy::kat_working` or `copy::you_editing` in the page's `lang`; `Bridge::from_location` gets the app's repaint as its `wake` (the table names where the web build holds its `egui::Context`); `Bridge::poll` runs once per frame. The test `tests/katwork_bridge.rs` checks `load_then_save_round_trips`, `read_only_refuses_edits` (an edit through each gated place changes neither the content nor `revision`, and a save request fails `read_only`) and `revision_counts_edits`; the in-browser job (Task 17) checks the storage, the policy and the round trip with each app's fixture document.
  - **Thai line breaking**, where the app lacks it: VectorCraft's approach (`crates/text/src/shape.rs:243-278` in VectorCraft): `icu_segmenter` 2.3; for text holding Thai, Lao, Khmer or Myanmar, `LineSegmenter::new_dictionary` on native builds and `LineSegmenter::new_auto` (the LSTM models) on wasm32 give the break opportunities inside those runs, other text keeps its own breaks, and no break splits a grapheme cluster.
  - **Thai fonts**: KatWork's bundled fonts (Task 5) are embedded at build time from `KATWORK_FONTS_DIR` when it is set (nothing is embedded without it, so an upstream build is unchanged) and are then the first Thai fallback in the native and web builds; a document that names TH Sarabun New or TH SarabunPSK gets Sarabun, any other missing Thai face Noto Sans Thai, and the app reports the substitution where it reports others. Each fork commits the same files under `katwork/fonts/` (OFL-1.1, with `OFL.txt` and a byte copy of Task 5's `FONTS.md`), and its `katwork.yml` job checks their SHA-256 against that `FONTS.md`, so the fork's own CI has the fonts without KatWork; the image build uses KatWork's own copy (`/fonts`).
  - **The Thai gate test**, `thai_gate`, on Task 18's fixture: each paragraph laid out with the embedded Sarabun at 16 pt in a box 160 pt wide gives at least four lines; every line ends at one of the fixture's boundaries or the paragraph's end; no line is wider than the box; no character maps to glyph 0. It is compiled only with its crate's `katwork-fonts` feature, whose build fails without `KATWORK_FONTS_DIR`, so an upstream `cargo test --workspace` never runs it and, when it runs, it never skips; in a fork it runs as `KATWORK_FONTS_DIR=$PWD/katwork/fonts cargo test -p <gate package> --features katwork-fonts --test thai_gate`. The image build runs it (Task 15).
  - **The marks rule** of Task 13 holds for every change, and the KatWork PR pins the fork's new commit, its `web_features` and its `thai_gate` in the lock, adds the app to `check-craft-lock.sh`'s `REQUIRE_THAI_GATE` list, and adds the app and one fixture document to Task 17's in-browser job (`web/craft-e2e/apps.json`).
  - **File and line references** in Tasks 13–22 point at the upstream code as read on 2026-10-24; when a fork base is newer, the builder finds the same code by the names given and notes the new places in the PR.
- **Builders** (R20). Each task's **Builder** line names the model that builds it: `Opus` for the trust core, `Sonnet` for the rest. A Sonnet task's steps leave no choice open; when a Sonnet builder meets a case its task does not decide, it stops and asks in the PR instead of choosing.

## Review Focus

1. **Hostile or oversized files.** A zip bomb dressed as DOCX, an XLSX whose central directory lies about sizes, XML with a `DOCTYPE` or 10,000 nested elements, an encrypted package, a PDF with a FlateDecode bomb in a page, a font, a ToUnicode CMap or an object stream, a page-tree cycle or 100 nested Form XObjects, a 5,000-page PDF, an `.exe` renamed `.pdf`, a 2 GB upload, and a file that compromises a Craft program, which then double-forks, forks until it hits its limit, writes to `/dev/shm`, hands a shared-memory segment to another uid, fills `/work`, looks for a descriptor of another session, unpacks a bundle with a link in it, or swaps its output for a symlink. Expected: a bounded error within the call timeout; other requests keep working; the document pool recovers its threads, one workspace's file never holds every thread, and a pool that stays wedged restarts the process; no output artifact row (a hostile package with the right magic bytes is accepted at upload and refused by every reader); nothing of a Craft call survives it, and no call reads another's files. (Task 2 `upload_over_cap_is_413_before_reading_the_body`, Task 3 `hostile_packages_fail_bounded`, `watchdog_exit_decision_table`, `one_workspace_cannot_hold_every_pool_thread`, Task 4 `xlsx_lying_central_directory_is_bounded`, Task 5 `pdf_flate_bomb_fails_bounded`, `pdf_font_and_cmap_bombs_fail_bounded`, `pdf_object_stream_budget_is_enforced_during_load`, `pdf_page_tree_cycle_fails_bounded`, `pdf_deep_xobject_nesting_fails_bounded`, Task 6 `read_file_hostile_file_returns_bounded_error`, Task 15 `a_double_forked_child_does_not_survive`, `fork_bomb_is_contained_and_killed`, `shared_memory_and_queues_are_refused`, `a_symlinked_output_is_refused`, `a_program_inherits_only_stdio`, `a_call_over_its_disk_cap_is_killed`; Task 16 `a_session_over_its_disk_cap_is_killed`, `bundles_round_trip_and_hostile_bundles_fail`; Task 40 `rasterize_hostile_pdf_is_bounded`.)
2. **Sources that are not sources.** A made-up span or citation id, one from another job, Space or workspace, a page or cell typed into a claim, a quote that starts or ends inside a figure or a date, a quote from the Kat's own earlier output, invisible, white (in any colour space), transparent, clipped, tiny or covered PDF text, PDF text in a paint the reader cannot resolve (a gradient or pattern fill, a soft mask, a blend mode other than Normal), PDF text under a shading or a translucent area drawn over it, inside a transparency group painted transparent, or over an image, a blended or translucent area or a shape that is not a rectangle, white characters inside an otherwise visible line, a hidden or white spreadsheet cell or rich-text run, white DOCX text on a page colour Word's print layout does not show or in a theme-coloured shading, an instruction planted in a file, a file replaced or deleted after citing, document text that imitates `read_file`'s own lines, a Craft app asked for the text of a file or input that holds hidden text (directly, inside a batch step, or through GridCraft's grid preview), or of one that holds parts the readers skip, or a Craft session whose own edits make hidden text visible or that saves such a file as CSV, PDF text covered inside a translucent or knockout transparency group, PPTX text whose size or paint is inherited or cannot be resolved. Expected: invalid-arguments errors for made-up and foreign ids, no field that takes a locator, hidden text (as the readers detect it, unresolved paint included; text covered only in part, or by several areas none of which covers its whole box, is a blind spot ADR-0010 names) never shown to the model, by `read_file` or by a Craft tool, and every other case leaves the claim `unverified` with its code (`source_generated`, `hidden_source`, `instruction_like_source`, `suspect_text_layer`, `stale`, `source_deleted`), never `passed`. (Task 3 `docx_page_colour_counts_without_the_display_setting`, `docx_theme_colours_of_shading_and_page_colour_resolve`; Task 4 `rich_text_white_run_is_hidden`; Task 5 `pdf_white_tiny_and_covered_text_is_hidden`, `pdf_transparency_groups_fold_into_their_content`, `pdf_hidden_characters_leave_their_visible_line`, `typst_white_text_is_low_contrast`, `pdf_unknown_paint_is_hidden`, `typst_gradient_text_is_hidden`, `pdf_text_over_an_image_or_shading_is_suspect`; Task 6 `span_lookup_other_space_or_workspace_is_not_found`, `read_file_block_text_cannot_forge_harness_lines`, `hidden_blocks_never_reach_the_model`; Task 16 `every_text_returning_rule_is_marked`, `text_calls_in_a_hidden_content_session_never_reach_the_app`, `error_replies_are_withheld_in_a_hidden_content_session`; Task 23 `hidden_content_session_refuses_text_tools`, `unreadable_file_makes_a_hidden_content_session`, `skipped_parts_make_a_hidden_content_session`, `a_file_saved_in_a_hidden_content_session_makes_one`, `grid_preview_facts_omit_hidden_cells`, `grid_preview_after_unhiding_edits_holds_no_hidden_text`; Task 24 `inherited_white_and_tiny_runs_are_hidden`, `unresolvable_or_no_fill_text_is_hidden`, `unsupported_choice_reads_the_fallback`; Task 25 `hidden_content_session_keeps_its_mode_after_a_save`, `flattening_saves_are_refused_in_a_hidden_content_session`, `receipts_in_a_hidden_content_session_hold_no_document_text`; Task 7 `cite_widens_a_quote_to_whole_numbers_and_dates`; Task 9 `foreign_or_unknown_citation_is_refused`; Task 12 `claim_citing_a_kat_output_cannot_pass`, `claim_citing_invisible_pdf_text_cannot_pass`, `claim_citing_an_instruction_like_span_cannot_pass`, `rich_text_hidden_run_never_supplies_a_value`, `tampered_source_marks_claims_stale`; Task 28 `hijacked_writer_cannot_pass_the_injected_figure`; Task 30 `claims_flip_to_source_deleted`; Task 36 `katbench_inject_invariants_hold`.)
3. **Thai and numeric variants, and numbers out of context.** Tone mark before or after a vowel, SARA AM as U+0E4D U+0E32, ZWSP inside words, Thai digits, mixed digits, number words, หมื่นล้าน and ล้านล้าน, a scale only in the table header, a `#,##0,,` cell format, accounting negatives, BE versus CE, two-digit years, fiscal years, rounding, the right number for the wrong entity (one letter apart) or year, a dropped negation or comparator, a number hidden in a `fact` claim. Expected: equal readings with their subject words, periods and qualifiers pass; different readings fail; a missing subject word, a different qualifier or an ambiguous reading is `unverified`, never passed or failed by a guess.
