    - EffectCraft `model_download_and_plugins_are_unknown`: `segment.model.download` and `effect.plugins.load` return the unknown-command error
    - VectorCraft and PhotoCraft `plugin_commands_are_unknown`: `plugin.install` with inline data returns the unknown-command error
  - In each gated fork, the CI job `katwork-profile`: `cargo tree -p <cli> -e normal --no-default-features --features <the lock's features> -i <gated crate>` and `cargo tree -p <web crate> --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -e normal --no-default-features --features <the lock's web_features> -i <gated crate>` each print that the package is not in the graph (for SoundCraft each of the three host crates; for PdfCraft `pdfcraft-js` and `boa_engine`; for FigCraft `figcraft-collab-net`, `tungstenite`, `rustls`, `figcraft-plugin` and `wasmi`; for LightCraft `lightcraft-fetch`, `rustls` and `webpki-roots`; for VectorCraft `vectorcraft-plugins` and `wasmi`; for PhotoCraft `photocraft-plugins` and `wasmi`; for EffectCraft `effectcraft-plugin` and `wasmi`), and the gate test above passes.
  - In KatWork, the CI job `craft-lock`: `scripts/check-craft-lock.sh --self-test`, then the check on the lock.
- [ ] **Step 2: Run** the two jobs on each fork's base. Expected: FAIL (marks found; the gated crates are in both graphs).
- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** the deletions, the brand lines, the gates, the xtask flags and `KATWORK.md`; open the upstream PRs for the gates; write the lock and its check in KatWork.
- [ ] **Step 4: Run** the same jobs, `scripts/check-craft-lock.sh --self-test` and the check, and each fork's own test suite (`cargo test --workspace`) with the KatWork features. Expected: PASS, and the upstream suite still passes with default features.
- [ ] **Step 5: Commit** in each fork with `chore(katwork): remove the ArtCraft marks` and `feat(katwork): <gate> feature, on by default`; in KatWork with `chore(craft): pin the Craft forks`.

### Task 14: The Craft forks: patent-pool codecs compiled out

**Size:** M (FilmCraft is most of it). **Trust core** (it removes decoders and encoders of hostile media from the sandbox, and decides what KatWork redistributes). **Builder:** Opus. Decisions: R23, R28 (ADR-0011's licence notes). Built after Task 13 in the same forks.

**Repositories:** `Minniesse/deckcraft`, `Minniesse/filmcraft`, `Minniesse/effectcraft`, `Minniesse/soundcraft`. FilmCraft lands first: EffectCraft takes FilmCraft's codec crates as git dependencies, and its fork points them at the FilmCraft fork's commit.

**Files:** in each fork, the `Cargo.toml` of the media crates and of the CLI and web crates that forward the gate, the `#[cfg(feature = "patent-codecs")]` sites, `tests/katwork_codecs.rs`, the `katwork-profile` CI job (Task 13) extended; in KatWork, the four lock rows.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 13's forks and `katwork-profile` job.
- Produces:
  - **The rule** (ADR-0011): KatWork's builds carry no decoder or encoder for a codec the app repositories implement without a patent or royalty statement: H.264, HEVC (and so HEIC), AAC, AC-3 or E-AC-3, ProRes, DNxHD or DNxHR, APV or MPEG-2 video. They keep VP8, VP9, AV1, Motion JPEG, Opus, Vorbis, FLAC, ALAC, MP3, PCM WAV and AIFF, and the still-image formats; the licensing claims third parties make over some of these (VP9 and AV1 included) join Founder action 6's legal read. PhotoCraft's and LightCraft's `heif` features stay off (Task 13).
  - **The gate**, `patent-codecs`, default on, in each fork:

    | App | The crates it makes optional | Where the KatWork build refuses |
    |---|---|---|
    | DeckCraft | `deckcraft-h264` and `deckcraft-hevc` in `deckcraft-media`, and symphonia's `aac` feature | probing or inserting such media gives "codec not available in this build" (media insertion is also forbidden by Task 16's policy) |
    | FilmCraft | `filmcraft-h264`, `-h264enc`, `-hevc`, `-aac`, `-ac3`, `-prores`, `-dnx`, `-apv`, `-mpeg2v`, and symphonia's `aac` feature, in `filmcraft-codecs` and `filmcraft-export` | `media_import` of such a file and an export to `h264`, `prores`, `dnxhr`, `apv`, `mxf-op1a`, `mxf-opatom` or `aac` give "codec not available in this build"; the export format list (`export --list-presets`) leaves them out |
    | EffectCraft | the same FilmCraft crates through the FilmCraft fork, and its own `effectcraft-hevcenc`, in `effectcraft-media` and `effectcraft-export` | importing such footage and the `h264`, `hevc` and `prores` render outputs give "codec not available in this build" |
    | SoundCraft | `soundcraft-video`'s H.264 and ProRes decoders, and symphonia's `aac` feature | importing such audio or video gives "codec not available in this build" |

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** in each fork's `tests/katwork_codecs.rs`, built with the gate off, on inputs generated in code (an MP4 whose sample entry is `avc1`, one with `hvc1`, an M4A with `mp4a`, a MOV with ProRes `apcn`, a WebM with VP9 and Opus):
  - `patent_codecs_are_refused_cleanly`: each `avc1`, `hvc1`, `mp4a` and `apcn` input fails with the "codec not available in this build" error, and the process keeps serving (the next request succeeds)
  - `royalty_free_codecs_still_work`: the VP9 and Opus WebM imports and probes (FilmCraft also exports a WAV and a FLAC of it)
  - FilmCraft and EffectCraft `export_formats_leave_out_patent_codecs`: the format list holds no `h264`, `hevc`, `prores`, `dnxhr`, `apv` or `aac` entry
  - the `katwork-profile` job: Task 13's two `cargo tree` forms, for the CLI with the lock's `features` and for the web crate on `wasm32-unknown-unknown` with its `web_features`, find none of the gated crates
- [ ] **Step 2: Run** the tests and the job on Task 13's commits. Expected: FAIL.
- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** the gate; open the upstream PRs; update the four lock rows in KatWork.
- [ ] **Step 4: Run** the same commands, and each fork's own suite with default features. Expected: PASS.
- [ ] **Step 5: Commit** in each fork with `feat(katwork): patent-codecs feature, on by default`; in KatWork with `chore(craft): pin the media forks without patent codecs`.

### Task 15: The `katwork-craft` image, built and signed by CI, and the sidecar's one-shot checks

**Size:** M. **Trust core** (native tools on hostile input; the image's provenance). **Builder:** Opus. Decisions: R10, R12, R14, R19, R22, R28. Grows the former sidecar task: all 13 CLIs, built by CI and signed.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/katwork-craft/{Cargo.toml,src/main.rs,src/lib.rs,src/api.rs,src/run.rs,src/sandbox.rs,src/uids.rs,src/verify.rs,src/disk.rs}`, `crates/katwork-craft/tests/{api.rs,sandbox.rs,verify.rs}`, `crates/katwork-craft/tests/fixtures/{self-test.docx,self-test.xlsx,self-test.json}`, `crates/katwork-craft/tests/fixtures/signing/{BUILD-HASHES.txt,BUILD-HASHES.sigstore.json,IDENTITY}` (the signed fixture, below), `docker/craft.Dockerfile`, `docker/craft-image.lock`, `docker/build-craft-cli.sh`, `docker/sigstore-trusted-root.json`, `docker-compose.craft-build.yml`, `.github/workflows/{craft-image,craft-fixture}.yml`, `crates/katwork/src/craft/{mod,client,apps}.rs`, `crates/katwork/tests/craft.rs`
- Modify: `Cargo.toml` (member), `docker-compose.yml` (the `katwork-craft` service, the `craft` network, `katwork` on it), `docker-compose.https.yml` and `docker-compose.demo.yml` (`katwork` keeps `craft` in its overridden networks), `docker-compose.smoke.yml` (`KATWORK_CRAFT: "off"`), `.env.example` and `scripts/init-env.sh` (`KATWORK_CRAFT_IMAGE`, `KATWORK_CRAFT_WORK_SIZE`), `crates/katwork/src/{lib,config,runtime}.rs`, `crates/katwork/src/types.rs` (`ReasonCode::CraftUnavailable`), `crates/katwork/tests/types.rs` and `web/src/i18n/i18n.test.ts` (the list they pin), `crates/katwork/src/error.rs` and `crates/katwork-server/src/error.rs` (`KatError::PreviewUnavailable`, 503 `preview_unavailable`), `crates/katwork/src/docs/tools.rs` (the GridCraft `XlsxCheck`), `crates/katwork/src/jobs/job.rs` (the outage notice), `crates/katwork/tests/support/mod.rs` and `crates/katwork-server/tests/support/mod.rs` (`KATWORK_CRAFT=off` by default, `with_craft_stub`), `crates/katwork-server/src/routes/{files,health}.rs` (the preview route, `craft` in `/healthz`), `crates/katwork-server/tests/e2e_compose.rs` (every profile keeps `craft`), `web/src/components/Transcript.test.tsx`, `web/src/i18n/{en,th}.json` (the notice and the preview error), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (jobs `craft-image`, `craft-sandbox` and `xlsx-check`), `NOTICE`, `README.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Tasks 13 and 14's fork commits and Task 13's lock (`docker/craft-binaries.lock`, its columns used unchanged); Task 9's writers and their `XlsxCheck` hook; Task 5's font files; SP1's `BoundedHttp`.
- Produces:
  - **The build.** `docker/build-craft-cli.sh <repo> <commit> <package> <bin> <features> <thai_gate>` (one lock row's columns) clones the repository at that commit (`git init`, `git fetch --depth 1 origin <commit>`, `git checkout FETCH_HEAD`), installs the toolchain its `rust-toolchain.toml` names (Rust 1.97.1 when it names none), runs the app's Thai gate test when the row names one (`<gate package>::<test>`: `cargo test --locked -p <gate package> --features katwork-fonts --test <test> -- --exact <test>` with `KATWORK_FONTS_DIR=/fonts`, which must print `test <test> ... ok`, so a misspelled gate cannot pass by running nothing and an image is never built from a fork whose Thai gate fails; `build-craft-cli.sh --self-test` feeds that check a log holding `test thai_gate ... ok` and one holding only `running 0 tests`, and exits 1 unless only the first passes), and runs `cargo build --release --locked -p <package> --no-default-features --features <features>` with `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0`, `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH=0`, `RUSTFLAGS=--remap-path-prefix=/src=/build`, `KATWORK_FONTS_DIR=/fonts` (Task 5's font files, which the forks embed, Tasks 18–22) and, for FigCraft, `FIGCRAFT_DEFAULT_SERVER=`. `docker/craft.Dockerfile` has one builder stage per app (a Rust image pinned by digest; BuildKit builds them in parallel and caches each), one for `katwork-craft`, and a runtime stage on `gcr.io/distroless/cc-debian12` pinned by digest: no shell, no package manager, no `curl`, `espeak`, `lp` or `fc-match`. It copies in the 13 CLIs to `/usr/lib/katwork-craft/bin/`, Task 5's fonts to `/usr/share/fonts/katwork/`, and `katwork-craft`, and its last build step writes `/usr/share/katwork-craft/BUILD-HASHES.txt`: one `<sha256>  <path>` line for **every regular file of the runtime filesystem**, the distroless base's loader, libraries and certificates included, sorted by path, leaving out only `/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev`, `/work`, the three files Docker mounts at run time (`/etc/hostname`, `/etc/hosts`, `/etc/resolv.conf`), the list itself and its bundle. Release assets are never downloaded; `craft-launcher` and every updater are absent.
  - **Built and signed by CI, pulled by digest** (R28). `.github/workflows/craft-image.yml`, on pushes to `main` that change `docker/craft-binaries.lock`, `docker/craft.Dockerfile`, `docker/build-craft-{cli,web}.sh` or `crates/katwork-craft/`, and by hand: builds the image with the GitHub Actions cache, extracts `BUILD-HASHES.txt` from it, signs it with `cosign sign-blob --yes --bundle BUILD-HASHES.sigstore.json` (Sigstore keyless, the workflow's OIDC identity), adds the bundle as the image's last layer, pushes `ghcr.io/minniesse/katwork-craft` and prints its digest, and opens a PR that writes `docker/craft-image.lock` (`image <ref>@sha256:<digest>`, `built_from <KatWork commit>`, `hashes_sha256 <SHA-256 of BUILD-HASHES.txt>`) and the `KATWORK_CRAFT_IMAGE` line of `.env.example` (a merge of that PR changes none of the trigger paths, so it starts no rebuild). The PR is opened with the workflow's `GITHUB_TOKEN`, so CI starts on it only once a maintainer approves its run or closes and reopens it; the coordinator does that before merging.
  - **A signed fixture for the verify test.** `.github/workflows/craft-fixture.yml`, on `pull_request` changes to `crates/katwork-craft/tests/fixtures/signing/BUILD-HASHES.txt` (with `permissions: {contents: read, id-token: write}`), signs that file with the same `cosign sign-blob --yes --bundle` command and uploads the bundle as a workflow artifact; it pushes no image and opens no PR. The builder commits the bundle as `BUILD-HASHES.sigstore.json` and the job's certificate identity, `https://github.com/Minniesse/katwork/.github/workflows/craft-fixture.yml@refs/pull/<n>/merge`, as `IDENTITY`. `cosign` comes from `sigstore/cosign-installer` pinned by commit SHA in both workflows. `docker-compose.yml` runs `image: ${KATWORK_CRAFT_IMAGE:?set KATWORK_CRAFT_IMAGE in .env, or run scripts/init-env.sh}` with **no** `build:` section, so `docker compose up --build` builds KatWork itself and pulls the Craft image by digest, rather than recompiling 13 apps on every start; `docker-compose.craft-build.yml` adds `build: {context: ., dockerfile: docker/craft.Dockerfile}`, `image: katwork-craft:local` and `KATWORK_CRAFT_ALLOW_LOCAL: "1"`, so `docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.craft-build.yml up --build` builds the same pinned commits on the host. (A digest-pinned image name cannot be a build tag, which is why the local build is an override file.) The README says the local build compiles 13 applications and takes a long time.
  - **Checked at startup** (R28). `katwork_craft::verify::verify_image(root_dir: &Path, hashes: &Path, bundle: Option<&Path>, root: &TrustedRoot, allow_local: bool) -> Result<ImageKind, VerifyError>` with `ImageKind::{Signed, Local}`: it re-hashes every file `BUILD-HASHES.txt` lists (a missing or different file is `VerifyError::HashMismatch { path }`) and walks `root_dir` (`/` in the image) without following links or leaving its filesystem, skipping the paths the list leaves out, so a regular file the list does not name is `VerifyError::Unlisted { path }`; when the bundle exists it verifies it against `docker/sigstore-trusted-root.json` (Sigstore's public-good trusted root, compiled in and pinned by SHA-256, refreshed by a CI job) with no network, requiring the certificate identity `https://github.com/Minniesse/katwork/.github/workflows/craft-image.yml@refs/heads/main` and issuer `https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com` (`VerifyError::Signature`); with no bundle it is `Ok(Local)` only when `allow_local` (`KATWORK_CRAFT_ALLOW_LOCAL=1`), else `VerifyError::Unsigned`. `katwork-craft serve` and `katwork-craft web` (Task 17) run it first: an error logs one line `craft_image_unverified: <reason>` and exits 78; `Local` logs one warning `katwork-craft: local build, the binary list is not signed`. The `sigstore` crate (Apache-2.0) verifies the bundle offline; if the builder finds it cannot verify a bundle without network, the task stops and asks in the PR (the fallback is the pinned `cosign verify-blob --offline` binary in the image).
  - `crates/katwork-craft`, a library and a thin binary (so its integration tests reach `api`, `uids` and `sandbox`) built only into its image; it never depends on `katwork`. Its root filesystem is read-only, with one tmpfs at `/work` (mode 0711, owned by root, `size=${KATWORK_CRAFT_WORK_SIZE:-1g}`). It is **not** a child process of the server. `katwork-craft healthcheck` exits 0 when the server answers `GET /v1/health`; `katwork-craft healthcheck --web` exits 0 when the web server of Task 17 answers `GET /host/?app=wordcraft` on port 3002 with 200. The process code (`sandbox.rs`, and Task 16's sessions) is `#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]`, with a stub on other systems whose calls return 503; there the binary builds and exits with `katwork-craft runs only on Linux`, so `cargo build --workspace` and `cargo test --workspace` (the `api.rs` tests included) still work on a developer's machine.
  - **Part of the default product stack.** `docker-compose.yml` runs `katwork-craft` (`command: ["serve"]`, `restart: unless-stopped`, `read_only: true`, `init: true` (it reaps the orphans a killed program leaves), `tmpfs: ["/work:mode=0711,size=${KATWORK_CRAFT_WORK_SIZE:-1g}"]` (a bound on everything the programs write, shared out by the disk caps below), `ipc: none` (a private IPC namespace with no `/dev/shm`, because Docker leaves `/dev/shm` writable under `read_only`), `pids_limit: 2048`, `cap_drop: [ALL]`, `cap_add: [SETUID, SETGID, CHOWN, DAC_OVERRIDE, KILL]`, `security_opt: ["no-new-privileges:true"]` with Docker's default seccomp profile left on (it blocks the kernel keyring calls), `mem_limit: ${KATWORK_CRAFT_MEMORY_LIMIT:-4g}`, a healthcheck on `katwork-craft healthcheck`) on the internal network `craft` (`internal: true`, no published port, no egress), and adds `craft` to `katwork`'s networks. `katwork` does **not** `depends_on` it: a sidecar outage must never stop the server (R19). `docker-compose.https.yml` and `docker-compose.demo.yml` override `katwork`'s networks, so each lists `craft` too; `docker-compose.smoke.yml` sets `KATWORK_CRAFT: "off"` and gives `katwork-craft` the Compose profile `craft`, so the smoke stack neither pulls nor starts it (Task 17 does the same for `katwork-craft-web`). Until the first published image, `.env.example` names `katwork-craft:local` and the README says to build it with the override file.
  - **Every process the sidecar starts runs as its own throwaway user** (R22). `katwork_craft::sandbox::Jail { uid: u32, gid: u32, root: PathBuf }` and `sandbox::spawn_jailed(argv: &[OsString], jail: &Jail, env: &[(String, String)], limits: &ProcLimits) -> io::Result<Child>`. The supervisor runs as uid 0 with only `SETUID`, `SETGID`, `CHOWN` and `DAC_OVERRIDE` (to create a directory for another uid, run a program as that uid and read its output) and `KILL` (signalling a process of another uid needs it, so a deadline can end the program). For each process it takes a fresh uid from `uids::UidAllocator::new(start: u32) -> UidAllocator` (the sidecar uses 1,000,000), `UidAllocator::retire(&mut self, uid: u32)` (for a uid that stays held, below) and `UidAllocator::next(&mut self, in_use: &dyn Fn(u32) -> bool) -> Option<u32>` (pure, compiled on every system): the next value of a counter from `start`, skipping every uid for which `in_use` is true (a process of that uid is in `/proc`, or a directory owned by it exists) and every retired uid. The counter never wraps: past 2,147,483,647, `next` is `None`; that takes about two billion processes, and a container restart, which ends every process and empties `/work`, starts it again; the gid equals the uid, and `None` is a 503. A host that remaps users (rootless Docker, `userns-remap`) maps only about 65,536 uids into a container, so `setuid` to these fails there: each call is then a 503 (logged once, `craft_uid_unmapped`), saves go on unchecked (R19), and the README says the sidecar needs a host that does not remap users. **Every program gets the same layout**, a one-shot call in `/work/c-<call id>/` and a session in `/work/s-<session id>/` (Task 16): the program's directory, root's with the program's gid and mode 0750, holding `in/` (the same owner and mode; each input root's, mode 0640, so the program can read and cannot change, rename or chmod it) and `out/`, `home/` and `tmp/` (the uid's, mode 0700); `HOME` and `TMPDIR` point into `home/` and `tmp/`, and a one-shot call's argv names `in/<name>` and `out/<name>`. The supervisor creates the directory, opens it as a directory fd before anything runs in it, creates the subdirectories with `mkdirat` and writes the inputs through `openat` relative to that fd (`O_CREAT | O_EXCL | O_NOFOLLOW`), and only then `fchown`s `out/`, `home/` and `tmp/` to the uid; `/work` is root's with mode 0711, so a program can neither list it nor replace its own directory. **Every descriptor the supervisor opens carries `O_CLOEXEC`** (directory fds, inputs, bundle entries, the removal walk, the output reads), and `spawn_jailed` closes every descriptor above 2 in the child before `exec` (`close_range(3, ~0U, 0)` in its `pre_exec`), so a program inherits only its stdin, stdout and stderr whatever a library does: never another program's directory or input, nor one of the supervisor's sockets. It starts the program **in its own session** (`setsid` in `pre_exec`, so no signal to a process group reaches the supervisor's) with that uid and gid, no supplementary groups, no capabilities, `PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS`, `oom_score_adj` 1000 (the kernel kills a program before the supervisor), the limits `ProcLimits { nofile: 1024, nproc: 128, fsize: 268_435_456, core: 0 }` (`RLIMIT_NPROC` counts per uid, so it bounds one program's threads and forks; at most 10 programs at once, 8 sessions and 2 one-shot calls, hold at most 1,280 of the container's `pids_limit` of 2048, leaving the rest for the supervisor, its waiting kill helpers and the healthcheck, so a fork bomb cannot starve them), and **a seccomp filter that refuses `socket(2)` and `socketpair(2)` for every address family, `AF_UNIX` included, `io_uring_setup`, `io_uring_enter` and `io_uring_register` (io_uring can create a socket without `socket(2)`), and every System V IPC and POSIX message-queue call** (`shmget`, `semget`, `msgget` and their siblings, `mq_open` and its siblings; `EPERM`; built with the `seccompiler` crate, Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause, for the image's architecture). **Other ABIs are refused before those rules**: the program `spawn_jailed` installs is a hand-written prologue followed by seccompiler's program, and the prologue loads `seccomp_data.arch` and returns `EPERM` unless it is the image's own audit architecture (so every call through the 32-bit compat ABI, `int 0x80` and its `socketcall` included, is refused), then loads `nr` and returns `EPERM` when bit 30 (`__X32_SYSCALL_BIT`) is set (x32 calls share x86-64's audit architecture and would miss rules keyed on native numbers). So no Craft program can open a network connection, to the `craft` network's other members included, reach another program through a socket, or share memory or a queue with another program. `AF_UNIX` is refused too because abstract Unix sockets share one namespace across the container, whatever the uid, so two sessions could meet through one; the apps' MCP servers create no socket (their Unix-socket code, the D-Bus theme and portal dialogs, single-instance locks and CUPS printing, belongs to the desktop apps, which the image does not hold, or to commands the policies forbid). The image's `--self-test` runs every app's session under this filter; if one needs a socket after all, the task stops and asks in the PR rather than allowing `AF_UNIX`. Argv is an explicit list (`Command::new`, never a shell), the environment is cleared and given an allowlist (`HOME` and `TMPDIR` inside the directory, `LANG=C.UTF-8`, `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=2`, and the app's own variables from Task 16's policy files), and the only writable paths are the program's own directories.
  - **Ending a program ends all of it.** Before a program starts, the supervisor starts its kill helper: its own binary run as `katwork-craft kill-helper <uid>` (a fresh process through `Command`, so nothing is forked from the threaded supervisor), which stays root, so the program cannot signal it, and waits on its stdin; a program that forks until it hits its limit therefore cannot stop the kill. When the program exits, is killed at its deadline, or its session closes, `sandbox::reap_uid(uid: u32, within: Duration) -> io::Result<()>` writes one byte to that stdin; the helper drops to the uid and sends `kill(-1, SIGKILL)`, which reaches every process of that uid (a double-forked child included) and no other, repeating it every 10 ms while a `/proc/<pid>/status` other than its own still shows that uid (the helper is a process of that uid itself once it drops to it; `init: true` reaps the orphans), for at most 5 s; it then removes every System V object (listed in `/proc/sysvipc/{shm,msg,sem}`) whose creator (`cuid`) or owner (`uid`) is that uid (`IPC_RMID` accepts the creator, so an object handed to another uid with `IPC_SET` goes too) and every POSIX message queue (`/dev/mqueue`) the uid owns, and exits. (The seccomp filter below refuses creating such objects at all; this sweep is the second line.) The supervisor waits for the helper to exit, reaping it, before it looks in `/proc` itself; a uid still held after that is retired, never handed out again, and the sidecar's health reports it. Only then is the directory removed and the uid freed. A session's close (Task 16) is this same `reap_uid` for the session's uid, never a process-group kill, so a child that left the program's session with `setsid` dies too.
  - **One program cannot fill `/work`.** Each session may hold at most `KATWORK_CRAFT_SESSION_DISK_BYTES` (default 268,435,456, from 134,217,728 to 1,073,741,824) in its directory, and each one-shot call 67,108,864. At startup the supervisor reads `/work`'s size (`statvfs`) and exits 78 with `craft_work_too_small` when `KATWORK_CRAFT_SESSIONS` × the session cap + 2 × the one-shot cap exceeds it (the README says to raise `KATWORK_CRAFT_WORK_SIZE` with the session count). `disk::usage(dir: &OwnedFd, stop_at_bytes: u64, stop_at_entries: u32) -> io::Result<Usage>` sums `st_blocks × 512` and counts entries by descriptor (`openat` and `fstatat` with `AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW`, as the removal walk does), stopping at either bound; the supervisor runs it on each program's directory after every call, preview and save and every 2 s while the program lives. A program over its cap, or over 100,000 entries, is killed (`reap_uid`), with one log line `craft_disk_limit <session or call id> <bytes>`: a one-shot call then answers 502 `craft_output_too_large`, and a session is dead (its next call 410 `craft_session_closed`).
  - **Reading a program's output never follows its links.** The supervisor reads an output with `openat2` relative to the program's directory fd, with `O_RDONLY | O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK | O_CLOEXEC` and `RESOLVE_BENEATH | RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS | RESOLVE_NO_MAGICLINKS`, then `fstat`: anything but a regular file owned by the program's uid with one link, within its cap, is refused (502 `craft_output_invalid`), a FIFO or a device without blocking, and the bytes are read from that fd only, so a link the program planted cannot make the supervisor, which holds `DAC_OVERRIDE`, read another program's files. Directories are removed through directory file descriptors (`openat` with `O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW | O_CLOEXEC`, then `unlinkat`), never following a link. Nothing is kept after a program ends.
  - **One-shot operations**, each with a 25 MiB body cap, a 30 s deadline that kills the program (or the caller's earlier `deadline_ms`), an output cap (PNG 8 MiB, JSON 1 MiB) and at most 2 at once (sessions, Task 16, have their own slots):
    1. `POST /v1/docx/preview?page=<n>`: `wordcraft-cli render <in>.docx <out>.png --page <n>` at 96 DPI;
    2. `POST /v1/xlsx/check`: `gridcraft-cli check --json <in>.xlsx`, returned as `{opened, would_repair, formula_cells, mismatched, no_oracle}`;
    3. `POST /v1/pdf/text`: PdfCraft's text extraction with page rectangles (`pdfcraft-cli run text_extract …`), returned as `{"pages": [{"page": u32, "width": f32, "height": f32, "lines": [{"text": string, "rect": [f32; 4]}]}]}`, where `rect` is `[x0, y0, x1, y1]` with `x0 ≤ x1` and `y0 ≤ y1`, in points from the page's top-left corner with y down, as PdfCraft's `text_lines` tool reports it, **disabled** unless `KATWORK_CRAFT_PDF_TEXT=on`, which only a later trust-core PR sets, if PdfCraft wins Task 39's challenge;
    4. `GET /v1/health`: `{"ok": true, "image": "signed" | "local", "hashes_sha256": <SHA-256 of BUILD-HASHES.txt>, "apps": [<app names in the image>]}`.

    Every other path outside `/v1/sessions` (Task 16) is 404. These argv forms are the only ones the one-shot path runs.
  - `katwork-craft --self-test` previews the committed `self-test.docx` and checks `self-test.xlsx`, exiting non-zero on a difference from `self-test.json`; Task 16 extends it to every app's session.
  - **Core.** `craft::apps::CraftApp`, the 13 apps with wire names `wordcraft`, `gridcraft`, `pdfcraft`, `deckcraft`, `designcraft`, `vectorcraft`, `figcraft`, `photocraft`, `lightcraft`, `cadcraft`, `filmcraft`, `effectcraft`, `soundcraft` (`wire()`, `from_wire()`, `display()`: "WordCraft" and so on; Task 23 adds their formats). `craft::client::CraftClient::new(base: Url, http: BoundedHttp) -> CraftClient`, with `preview_docx(&self, docx: &[u8], page: u32, deadline: Instant, cancel: &CancellationToken) -> Result<Vec<u8>, CraftError>`, `check_xlsx(&self, xlsx: &[u8], deadline, cancel) -> Result<XlsxCheckReport, CraftError>`, `pdf_text(&self, pdf: &[u8], deadline, cancel) -> Result<PdfTextReport, CraftError>` (used only by Task 39's bench) and `health(&self) -> CraftHealth` with `CraftHealth::{Ok { image: ImageKind, apps: Vec<CraftApp> }, Mismatch, Unavailable}`, cached for 30 s; Task 16 adds the session calls. **The running image must be the locked one**: KatWork compiles in `docker/craft-image.lock` (`include_str!`; the server image's build context holds it) and compares its `hashes_sha256` with the one `/v1/health` reports; a `signed` image whose list differs (an older signed image, or a rebuilt one the lock does not name) is `CraftHealth::Mismatch`, logged once as `craft_image_mismatch`, and every Craft call treats it as `Unavailable` (R19 for checks and previews, `craft_unavailable` for sessions); a `local` image is not compared, since it carries no signature to trust. `Config` gains `craft: CraftMode` (`KATWORK_CRAFT`: `on`, the default, or `off`; any other value is `ConfigInvalid { var: "KATWORK_CRAFT" }`), `craft_url` (`KATWORK_CRAFT_URL`, default `http://katwork-craft:8095`) and `craft_check_timeout` (`KATWORK_CRAFT_CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS`, default 10,000); `Core` gains `pub craft: Option<Arc<CraftClient>>` (`None` when off).
  - **Every XLSX output is checked before commit, and an outage never blocks a save** (R19). `GridcraftXlsxCheck`, the `XlsxCheck` the server registers when `craft` is `on`: a report with `opened: false` or `would_repair: true` is `Err(XlsxRefused::WouldBeRepaired)` (`xlsx_would_be_repaired`; nothing is committed); a clean report is `Ok(Passed)`; a connection failure, a timeout, a 5xx or a malformed reply is `Ok(Unavailable)`, and the version is saved with `gridcraft_check = 'unavailable'`. **The check has its own budget**, `craft::client::check_budget(now: Instant, tool_deadline: Instant, timeout: Duration) -> Option<Instant>`: the earlier of `now + timeout` and `tool_deadline − 3 s`, or `None` when that is not after `now`, in which case the hook returns `Ok(Unavailable)` without a request. With `craft` `off`, `NoXlsxCheck` (Task 9) gives `Off`, and the server logs one startup warning, `KATWORK_CRAFT=off: XLSX outputs are not checked by GridCraft`.
  - **The person is told.** `job::conclude` appends one localized System notice with code `craft_unavailable` (`ReasonCode::CraftUnavailable`; `ReasonCode::ALL` grows by one, and `tests/types.rs` and `i18n.test.ts` list it) when any version the run wrote has `gridcraft_check = 'unavailable'`; the transcript reads a notice's copy as `reason.<code>`, so its copy is `reason.craft_unavailable` (below). `/healthz` stays 200 with `"craft": "ok" | "unavailable" | "off"` and `"craft_image": "signed" | "local" | "mismatch" | "unknown"` in its body (the doc-pool wedge alone makes it 503). `GET …/spaces/{space_id}/files` carries each latest version's `gridcraft_check`, which the Files page shows (Task 29). Copy, exactly:

    | Key | en | th |
    |---|---|---|
    | `reason.craft_unavailable` | GridCraft could not check this workbook, so it was saved without the check. | GridCraft ตรวจสมุดงานนี้ไม่ได้ จึงบันทึกไว้โดยยังไม่ได้ตรวจ |
    | `reason.preview_unavailable` | The preview isn't available right now. | ตอนนี้ยังแสดงตัวอย่างไม่ได้ |

    `preview_unavailable` joins `error::API_CODES`.
  - **DOCX previews.** Route `GET /api/workspaces/{workspace_id}/files/{artifact_id}/versions/{version}/preview?page=<n>` (Kat outputs only; any workspace member): a PNG, cached as a derived blob under `<root>/<workspace_id>/derived/preview/<sha256>/<n>.png`, with `X-KatWork-Preview: approximate`. With the sidecar off or unavailable it is 503 `preview_unavailable`. The cache file is written inside a guarded transaction that holds the artifact row `FOR SHARE` through `store::lock_source_on` (the artifact lock contract), so a preview never outlives a deletion; Task 30's deletion removes these files.
  - **Test configs.** Both `tests/support/mod.rs` set `KATWORK_CRAFT=off` in `config()`, so no test needs a sidecar; `support::with_craft_stub(behaviour: StubCraft) -> (Config, StubHandle)` starts an in-process stub on a free port and sets `KATWORK_CRAFT=on` and `KATWORK_CRAFT_URL` to it. `StubCraft::{Clean, WouldRepair, Down, Hang}`; Task 16 adds the session behaviours.
  - CI job `craft-image`: builds `docker/craft.Dockerfile` with the layer cache and runs `katwork-craft --self-test` in it. **CI job `craft-sandbox`** runs the isolation tests as root inside the built image, with the service's exact settings. A last Dockerfile stage, `sandbox-test`, is the runtime stage plus the test binaries of `tests/sandbox.rs` and Task 16's `tests/session.rs` (built in the builder stage with `cargo test -p katwork-craft --release --no-run`, found through `--message-format=json` and copied to `/usr/local/bin/katwork-craft-{sandbox,session}-tests`), the stub program and Task 16's fake MCP server; only CI builds it. The job runs `docker build -f docker/craft.Dockerfile --target sandbox-test -t katwork-craft:sandbox-test .`, then, for each of the two test binaries, `docker run --rm --read-only --init --ipc none --pids-limit 2048 --tmpfs /work:mode=0711,size=1g --cap-drop ALL --cap-add SETUID --cap-add SETGID --cap-add CHOWN --cap-add DAC_OVERRIDE --cap-add KILL --security-opt no-new-privileges:true katwork-craft:sandbox-test <binary> --ignored --test-threads 1`, which must report every test passed and none filtered out. CI job `xlsx-check`: after the test suite, runs the image's `gridcraft-cli check --json` on every XLSX under `target/katwork-outputs/` (Task 9's writer tests) and in `demo/minutes-th/files/`; a file that fails to open, that GridCraft would repair, or whose cached values mismatch fails the job. rust_xlsxwriter files without formulas report "no oracle" by design, which passes. Task 34 adds the generator's worlds.
  - Licences: `NOTICE` lists the 13 apps (the Apache-2.0 branch of MIT OR Apache-2.0) and their bundled notices, and ADR-0011's per-app notes. PdfCraft's OCR weights (CC-BY-SA-4.0) are not built into the image.

### Task 16: Craft sessions and the MCP policy proxy

**Size:** M (if it runs past three days, the 13 policy files move to a second PR on the same branch, reviewed on its own). **Trust core** (the proxy, not the app, is the boundary: R25). **Builder:** Opus. Decisions: R22, R25, R33.

**Files:**
- Create: `crates/katwork-craft/src/{session,mcp,policy,proxy,paths,bundle}.rs`, `crates/katwork-craft/policy/{wordcraft,gridcraft,pdfcraft,deckcraft,designcraft,vectorcraft,figcraft,photocraft,lightcraft,cadcraft,filmcraft,effectcraft,soundcraft}.json`, `crates/katwork-craft/tests/{session,policy,proxy,paths}.rs`, `crates/katwork-craft/tests/fixtures/fake-mcp/` (a test MCP server whose tools and replies come from a JSON script), `crates/katwork-craft/tests/fixtures/canary/{canary.docx,canary.xlsx,canary.pdf,canary.pptx,MANIFEST.md}` (the canary check's documents, below)
- Modify: `crates/katwork-craft/src/{api,main,run,disk}.rs`, `crates/katwork/src/craft/client.rs` (the session calls), `crates/katwork/tests/support/mod.rs` and `crates/katwork-server/tests/support/mod.rs` (`StubCraft` session behaviours), `crates/katwork/Cargo.toml` and `crates/katwork-server/Cargo.toml` (`katwork-craft` as a dev-dependency, for `StubCraft`'s policy decisions), `docker-compose.yml` and `.env.example` (`KATWORK_CRAFT_SESSIONS`, `KATWORK_CRAFT_SESSION_IDLE_SECONDS`, `KATWORK_CRAFT_SESSION_MAX_SECONDS`, `KATWORK_CRAFT_SESSION_DISK_BYTES`), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (the `craft-image` job's session self-test, and `tests/session.rs` in the `craft-sandbox` job)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 15's image, `Jail`, `spawn_jailed`, `ProcLimits`, API and `CraftClient`.
- Produces:
  - **Sessions** (R22 applied per MCP session, R25). One stdio MCP process per session, under its own fresh uid and gid (Task 15's jail), in `/work/s-<session id>/` with Task 15's layout (`in/` root's and read-only to the session, `out/`, `home/` and `tmp/` the session's), its descriptors closed, in its own session, and under its disk cap (`KATWORK_CRAFT_SESSION_DISK_BYTES`). `HOME`, `TMPDIR`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_CACHE_HOME` and `XDG_DATA_HOME` point into `home/` and `tmp/`. At most `KATWORK_CRAFT_SESSIONS` sessions at once (1–8, default 2), beside Task 15's two one-shot slots; idle sessions close after `KATWORK_CRAFT_SESSION_IDLE_SECONDS` (default 600) without a call and every session after `KATWORK_CRAFT_SESSION_MAX_SECONDS` (default 3600). **Closing runs Task 15's `reap_uid` for the session's uid**, which ends every process of it wherever it moved, then removes the directory; nothing is kept. A sidecar restart loses every session.
  - **The session API**, beside Task 15's operations (bodies capped as stated, every call with `deadline_ms`, at most 120,000):
    - `POST /v1/sessions` `{"app": <wire name>, "author": string (1–100 chars), "deadline_ms"}` → 201 `{"session": uuid}`; at capacity 429 `craft_busy`; an app this image lacks 404 `craft_app_unavailable`. The supervisor keeps `author` in memory and gives it to the app only through the `open` and `new` recipes, over the MCP pipe: **no tenant data goes on a command line**, because every process in the container can read every other's `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`, whatever its uid (its `environ` and its pipes need the same uid).
    - `PUT /v1/sessions/{id}/in/{name}`: the raw bytes of one input (at most 26,214,400; all inputs at most 67,108,864, else 413), `name` one segment checked by `paths::check_segment` → 204; after `start` it is 409 `craft_session_started`, so no input arrives after KatWork's hidden-text check fixed the mode.
    - `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/start` `{"main": name | null, "template": name | null, "hidden_content": bool, "deadline_ms"}`: unpacks `main` when it is a `kwbundle` (below), starts the app's MCP server with its policy's launch line, runs the handshake, checks the policy against the app's `tools/list`, runs the policy's `open` recipe on `main` or its `new` recipe for `template`, and returns `{"main": logical path | null, "tools": [{"name", "signature", "description", "returns_text": bool}], "commands": [{"tool", "id", "signature", "returns_text": bool}]}`: only the tools and command ids the policy allows, each `signature` a one-line form built from its input schema (`open_document(path: string, password?: string)`), each description cut to 300 characters, and `returns_text` from its rule. **`hidden_content` sets the session's mode for its whole life** (R33; `SessionMode::HiddenContent` when true, else `Normal`): KatWork sets it when the file or an input holds hidden text (Task 23); `start` succeeds once per session, and no other request changes the mode, so a save does not clear it.
    - **Recipe steps are the sidecar's own calls**, not the Kat's: the `open`, `new`, preview and save recipes are not decided by the Kat's rules and take no `seq` (KatWork logs each as its `open`, `preview` or `save` action). They are the only calls that may name a path outside `in/` and `out/` (GridCraft's preview snapshot in `tmp/`) or run a command a Kat may not (`file.setAuthor`).
    - `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/call` `{"seq": u64, "tool", "arguments", "deadline_ms"}` → `{"seq", "decision": "allowed", "rule", "mutates": bool, "rev": {"before": u64, "after": u64}, "result": {"content": [..], "isError": bool}}`, `{"seq", "decision": "forbidden", "rule": string | null, "code"}`, where `code` is `craft_tool_forbidden`, `craft_command_forbidden`, `craft_path_forbidden` or `craft_argument_forbidden`, or, in a hidden-content session, `{"seq", "decision": "refused", "rule", "code": "craft_hidden_content"}` for a call that would return document text (R33). The app sees nothing of a forbidden or refused call. **`seq` is the session's call number**: 1 for its first call, then one more for each call the sidecar decides, forbidden and refused ones included; recipe steps take none, and KatWork keeps the same count (`next_call_seq`, Task 23). A `seq` other than the session's next is 409 `craft_seq_mismatch` and advances nothing; a call after `drain` is 409 `craft_session_frozen` (below); a second call while one runs is 409 `craft_session_busy`; a call past its deadline kills the process (several apps cannot cancel a call) and is 504 `craft_timeout`; an exited process or a protocol error is 502 `craft_app_failed`; both leave the session dead, and every later call is 410 `craft_session_closed`.
    - `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/preview` `{"page": u32 | null, "time": f64 | null, "deadline_ms"}` → the policy's preview recipe: `image/png` (at most 8 MiB, with `X-Craft-Pages` when the app reports a count), `application/json` for the `summary` kind (at most 1 MiB), or, for the `grid` kind, the session's workbook (`application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.spreadsheetml.sheet`, at most 52,428,800 bytes): the recipe saves it into the session's `tmp/`, the sidecar reads it as it reads every output (below) and removes it, and KatWork builds the grid from it with its own XLSX reader (Task 23), so the cells a Kat or a person sees are the ones KatWork's reader finds visible, not the ones the app reports.
    - `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/save` `{"format": <wire name>, "name": file name, "deadline_ms"}` → the policy's save recipe for that format writes `out/<name>`; the sidecar reads it as Task 15 reads every output (`openat2` beneath the session's directory with no symlinks, then a regular file owned by the session's uid), refusing anything else (502 `craft_output_invalid`) or a file over 52,428,800 bytes (502 `craft_output_too_large`), and returns its bytes with `X-Craft-Rev`. A `kwbundle` document is packed by `bundle::pack` (below) from `out/<stem>/` and the unchanged files of `in/<stem>/`, by descriptor.
    - `POST /v1/sessions/{id}/drain` `{"deadline_ms"}` **freezes the session**: from that moment every `call` is 409 `craft_session_frozen` before the proxy decides it (the preview and save recipes still run), and the reply comes once no call runs, `200 {"rev": u64, "next_seq": u64}` (or 504 at the deadline), so KatWork can tell whether every call the session ran was one it numbered. A session never unfreezes. `DELETE /v1/sessions/{id}` → 204. `GET /v1/sessions` → `[{"session", "app", "state", "frozen", "next_seq", "age_s", "idle_s"}]`.
  - **The MCP client.** `mcp::McpClient` speaks newline-delimited JSON-RPC 2.0 to the child: `initialize` with `protocolVersion` `2025-06-18`, `clientInfo {name: "katwork-craft"}` and empty capabilities (no roots, sampling or elicitation), then `notifications/initialized` and `tools/list` (following `nextCursor`). It sends the app no other method: `resources/*` and `prompts/*`, which several apps answer with document text, are never requested, and a Kat reaches only `tools/call` through the proxy. Lines over 16 MiB, a reply to an unknown id or malformed JSON are protocol errors. **A JSON-RPC error answer to `tools/call`** (`error {code, message, data}`, which several apps send instead of an `isError` result) becomes a tool result with `isError: true`: in a hidden-content session `{"content": [], "isError": true}`, like every error reply, and in a normal session one text item holding `message` (`data` is always dropped). **A request from the server** (`sampling/createMessage`, `elicitation/create`, `roots/list` or any other) gets JSON-RPC error -32601 and is never fulfilled: no Craft app can make KatWork run a model. Notifications are ignored. The child's stderr is discarded (its byte count is logged, never its text, because logs never hold file text).
  - **The launch line** comes only from the policy file, and `policy::check_launch(argv: &[String], env: &[(String, String)]) -> Result<(), PolicyError>` refuses any of `--connect`, `--bridge`, `--control`, `--attach`, `--launch`, `--lazy` and `--gpu`, a VectorCraft line without `--headless`, and any placeholder in the line or its environment other than `{session}`, `{in}`, `{out}` and `{home}` (so `{author}` can never reach a command line). No desktop app or control port exists in the image. **The roots are confirmed** (spec §19 item 7): a policy whose launch line passes automation roots names the app's `confined` stderr prefix (`wordcraft-cli: files confined:`, `pdfcraft-cli: files confined:`, `deckcraft-cli: files confined:`, `designcraft-cli: files confined:`, `vectorcraft-cli: files confined:`, `file access confined to ` for FigCraft, `soundcraft-cli: files confined:`; `null` for PhotoCraft and EffectCraft, which print none), and `start` fails with 502 `craft_app_failed` unless a stderr line with that prefix arrives before the handshake's reply and names, as its read and write roots, exactly the session's `in/` and `out/` directories (a line naming other roots fails the same way); it is the only stderr line read.
  - **The policy** (codex execpolicy style). Each app's `policy/<app>.json`, compiled in with `include_str!`, holds `{app, version, launch {argv, env, confined}, document, open, new {templates: {<template name>: [recipe step]}}, preview {kind, tool, arguments}, saves {<format>: {tool, arguments}}, rules: [Rule]}`. A `Rule` is `{id, tool, decision: "allow" | "forbidden", justification, mutates: bool, returns_text: bool, paths: {<JSON pointer>: "read" | "write"}, required: [JSON pointer], args: [{pointer, one_of | equals}], free: [JSON pointer], command: {pointer, params} | null, steps: {pointer, command, params} | null, commands: [CommandRule] | null, examples: {match: [args], not_match: [args]}}`, and a `CommandRule` is `{id, decision, justification, mutates, returns_text, paths, required, args, free, examples}`. A pointer names a property and everything beneath it; a `*` segment matches every element of an array (LightCraft's `import` declares `/paths/*` as a read path, PdfCraft's `doc_combine` `/inputs/*`). `free` lists the content fields a rule takes as they are (a `text`, a range's `values`); **arguments are closed-world**: a property no `paths`, `required`, `args`, `free`, `command` or `steps` pointer names is refused. `Policy::load` refuses a `free` or `args` pointer whose last key is path-like (below), so a path can never be declared free text (`PolicyError::PathLikeFree { rule, pointer }`). **`returns_text`** (R33) is true for every rule and command rule whose successful reply can carry document text: text the file holds or embeds (paragraphs, runs, cells and formula results, story, slide, shape, node and layer text, comments, notes, form values, captions, transcripts), the names its author gave things (sheets, styles, layers, nodes, tracks, clips), the file names it stores and its properties, in the result itself or in its warnings; a reply of ids, counts, geometry, revisions or an image is not text. Error replies are covered apart: several apps quote the document in an error (GridCraft a protected range's title or a sheet name, CADCraft a line of the file), so **in a hidden-content session the proxy withholds the content of every error reply** (`isError: true`), which reaches KatWork as `{"content": [], "isError": true}`, whatever the rule. Every rule and command rule must state it (a missing field fails `Policy::load` with `PolicyError::MissingField { rule, field: "returns_text" }`), and the table below names the ones that are true. `policy::Policy::load(app: &str, json: &str) -> Result<Policy, PolicyError>`, `Policy::self_test(&self) -> Result<(), PolicyError>` and `Policy::decide(&self, tool: &str, arguments: &Value, mode: SessionMode) -> Verdict` with `SessionMode::{Normal, HiddenContent}` and `Verdict { decision: Decision::{Allow, Forbidden, Refused}, rule: Option<String>, code: Option<&'static str>, mutates: bool, returns_text: bool }`. `decide`, in order:
    1. no rule names the tool: `Forbidden`, `craft_tool_forbidden` (**unknown tools are forbidden**); a `forbidden` rule: the same with its id;
    2. an `args` constraint fails (its pointer is present and its value is not one of, or not equal to, the stated ones), or a `required` pointer that is not a path pointer is absent: `craft_argument_forbidden` (WordCraft's `review.trackChanges` rule requires `/value`, so `{}` cannot toggle tracking off);
    3. a path pointer holds anything but a logical path of its mode, or a `required` path pointer is absent (so **a save without a path is refused**): `craft_path_forbidden`; and **any string under a path-like key that no path pointer declares** is `craft_path_forbidden` too, wherever it sits: a key that, compared case-insensitively, is `path`, `paths`, `file`, `files`, `dir`, `folder`, `input`, `output`, `out`, `src`, `dest`, `filename`, `url` or `uri`, or ends in `path`, `paths`, `file`, `files`, `dir`, `folder`, `root`, `url` or `uri` (`filePath`, `outputPath`, `image_path`, `saveDir`);
    4. **a property the rule does not name** (step 3's pointers, `args` and `free`, the `command` or `steps` pointer), at the top level of the arguments: `craft_argument_forbidden`, so an extra flag, mode or second command key the app would honour never reaches it;
    5. a command tool (`command` set) or a batch tool (`steps` set): each command id must name an `allow` command rule (**unknown commands are forbidden**, `craft_command_forbidden`), whose own `paths`, `required`, `args` and `free` apply to its params, closed-world as in steps 3 and 4; one forbidden step forbids the whole call;
    6. **in a `HiddenContent` session** (R33): when the rule is `returns_text`, or any command of the call (any step of a batch) has a `returns_text` command rule, `Refused`, `craft_hidden_content`, with the rule's id, so one text-returning step refuses the whole batch;
    7. otherwise `Allow`, with the rule's (or, for a batch, any step's) `mutates` and `returns_text`.

    `self_test` runs every rule's and command rule's examples through `decide` in both modes: each `match` example must get that rule's decision and id in a `Normal` session, and in a `HiddenContent` session the same, except that an allowed example whose rule, or one of whose commands, is `returns_text` must get `Refused` with `craft_hidden_content`; each `not_match` example must not be allowed under that rule's id; and every rule needs at least one `match` example. The sidecar runs it for all 13 apps at startup; a failure logs `craft_policy_invalid <app> <rule>` and exits 78. At each session's start, every `allow` rule's tool must be in the app's `tools/list` and every pointer's first key a property of that tool's input schema, else the session fails with 502 `craft_policy_mismatch` (an image whose fork renamed a tool). `katwork-craft --self-test` also starts one session per app, checks every allowed command id against the app's own command list, runs each `new` template (or, for PdfCraft and LightCraft, which have none, the `open` recipe on a small file of their format), saves the result in the app's document format and opens that file again in a second session (`session ok <app>`; for SoundCraft this is the `.scraftz` round trip of Task 21). **The canary check** (R33) backs the `returns_text` table with the apps' real replies: for the six apps that can open or take a file KatWork reads (WordCraft, GridCraft, PdfCraft, DeckCraft, VectorCraft on a PDF, and DesignCraft on a new document given `canary.docx` and `canary.pdf` as inputs and placing them), the self-test uses the compiled-in `canary.<docx|xlsx|pdf|pptx>`, every text of which (body, tables, headers, footnotes, comments, sheet names, slide notes, document properties) holds `KW-CANARY-7Q`, and calls **every allowed rule and command rule, mutating ones included, each in a fresh session** on that document, with its first `match` example. A reply holding `KW-CANARY-7Q` to a rule not marked `returns_text` fails the self-test with `craft_policy_text_unmarked <app> <rule>`; the builder then marks that rule `returns_text`, so the table below is a floor. What it cannot reach, ADR-0011 names: the apps whose files KatWork does not read, whose marks rest on their reply code as read at the fork base, and text a reply carries only in a state the first example does not produce (an undo label after a particular edit, a warning only some files raise). The canary files are made by hand-written OOXML (as Task 3's `ooxml` helper writes it) and the Typst CLI, and `MANIFEST.md` lists each one's SHA-256 and maker.
  - **Paths.** `paths::LogicalPath::parse(raw: &str, mode: PathMode) -> Result<LogicalPath, PathError>` with `PathMode::{Read, Write}`: `in/…` or `out/…` (reads), `out/…` only (writes); 2 to 6 segments, each NFC, 1–255 bytes, not `.` or `..`, with no `/`, `\`, NUL, control or format character (`paths::check_segment`). The proxy rewrites each logical path to the absolute one under the session's directory before the call, and rewrites the session directory's absolute prefix in every string of the result back to the logical form, so neither the Kat nor the model ever sees a uid or a session directory.
  - **Bundles.** `bundle::unpack(bytes: &[u8], into: &OwnedFd, caps: BundleCaps) -> Result<BundleManifest, BundleError>` and `bundle::pack(out_dir: &OwnedFd, base: &OwnedFd, manifest: &BundleManifest, uid: u32) -> Result<Vec<u8>, BundleError>`, both on directory descriptors. A `kwbundle` (media type `kwbundle`, Task 23) is a stored-only zip with `kwbundle.json` `{"app": wire name, "main": relative path, "files": [{"path", "sha256", "bytes"}]}` first; entries are checked like Task 3's package guard (no `..`, no absolute or duplicate names, at most 2,000 entries and 67,108,864 bytes, each entry's SHA-256 equal to the manifest's), and **an entry whose Unix mode is anything but a regular file or a directory** (a symlink, a FIFO, a device) is `BundleError::EntryType`. `unpack` writes into `in/<stem>/` only with `mkdirat` and `openat(O_CREAT | O_EXCL | O_NOFOLLOW | O_CLOEXEC)` beneath the session's directory fd, never through a path, so it creates nothing but root-owned regular files (mode 0640) and directories (0750). `pack` walks `out/<stem>/` and `in/<stem>/` by descriptor without following links and reads each file with Task 15's `openat2` rule (a regular file with one link, within the caps; owned by the session's `uid` in `out/`, by root in `in/`), so a link the app planted is 502 `craft_output_invalid` and is never followed. It is the document of the apps whose projects are several files (LightCraft, FilmCraft, EffectCraft, below).
  - **The 13 policies**, from each app's own MCP schema and command registry, written as data in `policy/<app>.json`. Every tool or command not listed as allowed is forbidden; the forbidden column names the notable ones each policy also lists with a justification and examples, so a reviewer sees them refused on purpose:

    | App | Launch (`{session}`, `{in}`, `{out}` and `{home}` are filled by the sidecar) | Allowed tools | Allowed commands, through its command and batch tools | Forbidden, notably |

    | WordCraft | `wordcraft-cli mcp --automation-read-root {in} --automation-write-root {out}` (the author comes from the `open` and `new` recipes, not this line) | `list_commands`, `get_text`, `inspect_document`, `render_page`, `open_document`, `save_document` (`.docx` or `.pdf`), `new_document` (`blank`, `letter`, `report`, `resume`), `type_text`, `select_text`, `execute`, `batch` (no `keepGoing`) | `text.insert`, `text.newParagraph`, `para.style`, `para.set`, `format.set`, `format.bold`, `insert.table`, `insert.picture` (path read), `references.toc`, `references.footnote`, `references.citation`, `references.bibliography`, `review.trackChanges`, `review.newComment`, `document.text`, `document.inspect` | `click`, `key`, `screenshot`, `ui_inspect` (they need a desktop app); `review.readAloud` (runs a speech program), `insert.object`, `insert.textFromFile`, `mailings.*`, `file.rename`, `file.saveTemplate`, `file.printPdf`, `review.restrict`, `tools.recordMacro`, `tools.updateStyles`, `styles.organizer`, `styles.copyFrom`, `file.setAuthor` (a Kat cannot change the name its tracked changes carry), `ui.*`; the `zotero` subcommand is never run |
